  "old_string": "/// `CLOCK_BOOTTIME` minus `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` elapsed divergence\n/// (seconds) at or above which [`warn_invalid`] reports that the\n/// system suspended during the run.\nconst SUSPEND_WARN_S: f64 = 1.0;",
  "new_string": "/// `CLOCK_BOOTTIME` minus `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` elapsed divergence\n/// (seconds) at or above which [`warn_invalid`] reports that the\n/// system suspended during the run.\nconst SUSPEND_WARN_S: f64 = 1.0;\n\n/// Band boundaries: familiar deciles in the body, nines/zeros\n/// notation in both tails — `nK`/`zK` mark the boundary with a\n/// fraction 10^-K of samples above (n) or below (z), so\n/// n2 ≡ p99, n3 ≡ p99.9, … n10, and z2 ≡ p1, z3 ≡ p0.1, z4.\n/// \"K nines\" is standard engineering shorthand for proportions\n/// near one (nines = -log10(1-x)); zK is this project's mirror\n/// of it for the fast tail. The slow tail goes deeper because a\n/// latency distribution is floored below (nothing beats the\n/// fast path) and open above. Deep bands populate only when the\n/// run has enough samples; empty bands are skipped.\nconst BOUNDARY_PCTS: &[f64] = &[\n    0.0,\n    0.0001,\n    0.001,\n    0.01,\n    0.10,\n    0.20,\n    0.30,\n    0.40,\n    0.50,\n    0.60,\n    0.70,\n    0.80,\n    0.90,\n    0.99,\n    0.999,\n    0.9999,\n    0.99999,\n    0.999999,\n    0.9999999,\n    0.99999999,\n    0.999999999,\n    0.9999999999,\n    1.0,\n];\n\n/// zpn-style name per boundary in [`BOUNDARY_PCTS`].\nconst BOUNDARY_NAMES_ZPN: &[&str] = &[\n    \"min\", \"z4\", \"z3\", \"z2\", \"p10\", \"p20\", \"p30\", \"p40\", \"p50\", \"p60\", \"p70\", \"p80\", \"p90\", \"n2\",\n    \"n3\", \"n4\", \"n5\", \"n6\", \"n7\", \"n8\", \"n9\", \"n10\", \"max\",\n];\n\n/// Literal-fraction name per boundary in [`BOUNDARY_PCTS`],\n/// `_`-grouped in threes after the decimal point so deep nines\n/// stay countable at a glance.\nconst BOUNDARY_NAMES_FRAC: &[&str] = &[\n    \"min\",\n    \"0.000_1\",\n    \"0.001\",\n    \"0.01\",\n    \"0.10\",\n    \"0.20\",\n    \"0.30\",\n    \"0.40\",\n    \"0.50\",\n    \"0.60\",\n    \"0.70\",\n    \"0.80\",\n    \"0.90\",\n    \"0.99\",\n    \"0.999\",\n    \"0.999_9\",\n    \"0.999_99\",\n    \"0.999_999\",\n    \"0.999_999_9\",\n    \"0.999_999_99\",\n    \"0.999_999_999\",\n    \"0.999_999_999_9\",\n    \"max\",\n];\n\n/// Band-label style for the report's histogram rows, selected by\n/// the `--band-labels` CLI flag.\n///\n/// - `Zpn` — nines/zeros + decile names (`z3`, `p50`, `n4`).\n/// - `Frac` — literal boundary fractions with `_` grouping\n///   (`0.001`, `0.50`, `0.999_9`).\n/// - `Both` — zpn and fraction side by side (`n4  0.999_9`);\n///   the default: the juxtaposition teaches the zpn vocabulary.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, clap::ValueEnum)]\npub enum BandLabels {\n    Zpn,\n    Frac,\n    Both,\n}\n\nimpl BandLabels {\n    /// Lowercase name used on the CLI and in the report header's\n    /// `labels=` metadata.\n    pub fn as_str(self) -> &'static str {\n        match self {\n            BandLabels::Zpn => \"zpn\",\n            BandLabels::Frac => \"frac\",\n            BandLabels::Both => \"both\",\n        }\n    }\n}"
}

The `adjusted` columns\n/// subtract per-call apparatus overhead\n/// (`overhead.per_call_ns(inner)`); the untrimmed `stdev` is the\n/// hdrhistogram-native stdev, which includes the ms-scale outliers\n/// in the tail band. Ends with `WARNING` lines flagging poisoned\n/// stats when they apply — `suspended_s` comes from\n/// [`run_adaptive`] (see [`warn_invalid`]).\npub fn print_report(\n    name: &str,\n    outer: u64,\n    inner: u64,\n    duration_s: f64,\n    hist: &Histogram<u64>,\n    overhead: &Overhead,\n    suspended_s: f64,\n) {\n    // Header line: bench name + logfmt-style metadata. `adj` is the\n    // apparatus overhead subtracted from each sample downstream.\n    let adj = overhead.per_call_ns(inner);\n    let total = outer * inner;\n    println!(\n        \"{name} [duration={:.1}s outer={} inner={} calls={} adj/call={}ns]:\",\n        duration_s,\n        fmt_commas(outer),\n        inner,\n        fmt_commas(total),\n        fmt_commas_f64(adj, 2),\n    );\n\n    // Band boundaries defined by percentiles. Each consecutive pair\n    // forms one band; we iterate the histogram to compute per-band\n    // stats (first, last, count, mean).\n    // Band boundaries: familiar deciles in the body, nines/zeros\n    // notation in both tails — `nK`/`zK` mark the boundary with a\n    // fraction 10^-K of samples above (n) or below (z), so\n    // n2 ≡ p99, n3 ≡ p99.9, … n10, and z2 ≡ p1, z3 ≡ p0.1, z4.\n    // \"K nines\" is standard engineering shorthand for proportions\n    // near one (nines = -log10(1-x)); zK is this project's mirror\n    // of it for the fast tail. The slow tail goes deeper because a\n    // latency distribution is floored below (nothing beats the\n    // fast path) and open above. Deep bands populate only when the\n    // run has enough samples; empty bands are skipped.\n    let boundary_pcts: &[f64] = &[\n        0.0,\n        0.0001,\n        0.001,\n        0.01,\n        0.10,\n        0.20,\n        0.30,\n        0.40,\n        0.50,\n        0.60,\n        0.70,\n        0.80,\n        0.90,\n        0.99,\n        0.999,\n        0.9999,\n        0.99999,\n        0.999999,\n        0.9999999,\n        0.99999999,\n        0.999999999,\n        0.9999999999,\n        1.0,\n    ];\n    let boundary_names: &[&str] = &[\n        \"min\", \"z4\", \"z3\", \"z2\", \"p10\", \"p20\", \"p30\", \"p40\", \"p50\", \"p60\", \"p70\", \"p80\", \"p90\",\n        \"n2\", \"n3\", \"n4\", \"n5\", \"n6\", \"n7\", \"n8\", \"n9\", \"n10\", \"max\",\n    ];\n\n    // Trim anchor: bands at or above the p99 boundary are the\n    // \"tail\" — excluded from the trimmed min..n2 stats no matter\n    // how many finer tail bands subdivide them.\n    #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n    // OK: 0.99 is a literal member of boundary_pcts above\n    let trim_bands = boundary_pcts.iter().position(|&b| b == 0.99).unwrap();\n\n    let n_bands = boundary_pcts.len() - 1;",
  "new_string": "/// row (empty bands are skipped). Label style comes from\n/// `cfg.band_labels` and is recorded as `labels=` in the header\n/// metadata so saved outputs are self-describing. The `adjusted`\n/// columns subtract per-call apparatus overhead\n/// (`cfg.overhead.per_call_ns(inner)`); the untrimmed `stdev` is the\n/// hdrhistogram-native stdev, which includes the ms-scale outliers\n/// in the tail band. Ends with `WARNING` lines flagging poisoned\n/// stats when they apply — `suspended_s` comes from\n/// [`run_adaptive`] (see [`warn_invalid`]).\npub fn print_report(\n    name: &str,\n    outer: u64,\n    inner: u64,\n    duration_s: f64,\n    hist: &Histogram<u64>,\n    cfg: &RunCfg,\n    suspended_s: f64,\n) {\n    // Header line: bench name + logfmt-style metadata. `adj` is the\n    // apparatus overhead subtracted from each sample downstream.\n    let adj = cfg.overhead.per_call_ns(inner);\n    let total = outer * inner;\n    println!(\n        \"{name} [duration={:.1}s outer={} inner={} calls={} adj/call={}ns labels={}]:\",\n        duration_s,\n        fmt_commas(outer),\n        inner,\n        fmt_commas(total),\n        fmt_commas_f64(adj, 2),\n        cfg.band_labels.as_str(),\n    );\n\n    // Row label for the band ending at boundary `i + 1`, per the\n    // selected style. min/max carry no fraction, so `both` prints\n    // them bare; elsewhere `both` pads zpn to its 3-char max so\n    // the fraction column aligns.\n    let band_label = |i: usize| -> String {\n        let zpn = BOUNDARY_NAMES_ZPN[i + 1];\n        let frac = BOUNDARY_NAMES_FRAC[i + 1];\n        match cfg.band_labels {\n            BandLabels::Zpn => zpn.to_string(),\n            BandLabels::Frac => frac.to_string(),\n            BandLabels::Both if zpn == frac => zpn.to_string(),\n            BandLabels::Both => format!(\"{zpn:<3} {frac}\"),\n        }\n    };\n    // Trimmed-stat row prefix labels, matching the band-label\n    // style so `n2` never appears in a frac-only table.\n    let trim_label = match cfg.band_labels {\n        BandLabels::Frac => \"min..0.99\",\n        BandLabels::Zpn | BandLabels::Both => \"min..n2\",\n    };\n    let mean_trim_label = format!(\"mean {trim_label}\");\n    let stdev_trim_label = format!(\"stdev {trim_label}\");\n\n    // Trim anchor: bands at or above the p99 boundary are the\n    // \"tail\" — excluded from the trimmed min..n2 stats no matter\n    // how many finer tail bands subdivide them.\n    #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n    // OK: 0.99 is a literal member of BOUNDARY_PCTS\n    let trim_bands = BOUNDARY_PCTS.iter().position(|&b| b == 0.99).unwrap();\n\n    let n_bands = BOUNDARY_PCTS.len() - 1;"
}

- `src/main.rs` — `--band-labels` flag (ValueEnum, default `both`), plumbed into `RunCfg`.
- 16 bench files — mechanical `cfg.overhead,` → `cfg,` at the `print_report` call.
- `README.md` — flag documented; `Cargo.toml`/lock → `0.14.0-1`.

Verified live: default `both` shows `n4  0.999_9` style labels; `zpn` and `frac` each render alone; `frac`'s trimmed rows read `min..0.99`; header carries `labels=zpn|frac|both`. fmt/clippy/test clean (15 tests), installed. Try it out — `iiac-perf zcr-spin-2t -d 5 --band-labels frac` etc. — and tell me when to mark done and write the description.

> DEVELOPER

How hard to generate BOUNDARIY_NAMES_XXX from BOUNDARIY_PCTS?

> AGENT

Easy — and there's a version that's *better* than deriving names from the floats: derive **both** the floats and the names from a tiny structural description, so nothing ever parses a float back into a name.

The insight is that the boundary set is already generative: `min`, then z-tail K = 4→2, then deciles 1→9, then n-tail K = 2→10 (0.99 *is* `n2`, so the high loop starts at 2), then `max`. About 50 lines total:

```rust
struct Boundary { pct: f64, zpn: String, frac: String }

fn boundaries() -> Vec<Boundary> {
    let mut v = vec![Boundary { pct: 0.0, zpn: "min".into(), frac: "min".into() }];
    for k in (2..=Z_DEPTH).rev() {                       // z4, z3, z2
        v.push(Boundary { pct: 10f64.powi(-k), zpn: format!("z{k}"), frac: frac_low(k) });
    }
    for d in 1..=9 {                                     // p10 .. p90
        v.push(Boundary { pct: d as f64 / 10.0, zpn: format!("p{d}0"), frac: format!("0.{d}0") });
    }
    for k in 2..=N_DEPTH {                               // n2 (=0.99) .. n10
        v.push(Boundary { pct: 1.0 - 10f64.powi(-k), zpn: format!("n{k}"), frac: frac_high(k) });
    }
    v.push(Boundary { pct: 1.0, zpn: "max".into(), frac: "max".into() });
    v
}
```

with `frac_high(k)` = `"0."` + k nines `_`-grouped in threes, `frac_low(k)` = `"0."` + (k−1 zeros + `"1"`) grouped — pure string building, no `log10`, no float-printing edge cases. The trim anchor becomes "index of the `n2` boundary" instead of a float equality compare (kills that `#[allow(unwrap_used)]` float-match), and the parallel-arrays test becomes structurally impossible to fail — replaced by a test pinning the exact rendered names against today's literals.

Payoffs beyond deduplication: tail depth becomes two constants (`Z_DEPTH`, `N_DEPTH`) — deepening the fast tail or adding a `--bands-depth` option later is a one-line change; and the three 23-element literal arrays (~70 lines) collapse. Cost: one `Vec` allocation per report (nothing at bench scale) and slightly more indirection when reading the code — you see the generator, not the table. A test printing the generated table keeps it inspectable.

The one design constant to keep an eye on: `10f64.powi(-k)` and `1.0 - 10f64.powi(-k)` reproduce today's literal values bit-for-bit at these magnitudes (and the band-assignment compare is `mid_rank < b`, so even a 1-ulp wobble at `n10` couldn't misroute anything measurable).

Since `-1` is still uncommitted and this replaces code `-1` just touched, I'd fold it in rather than churn the arrays twice. Want me to?

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/harness.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Band boundaries: familiar deciles in the body, nines/zeros\n/// notation in both tails — `nK`/`zK` mark the boundary with a\n/// fraction 10^-K of samples above (n) or below (z), so\n/// n2 ≡ p99, n3 ≡ p99.9, … n10, and z2 ≡ p1, z3 ≡ p0.1, z4.\n/// \"K nines\" is standard engineering shorthand for proportions\n/// near one (nines = -log10(1-x)); zK is this project's mirror\n/// of it for the fast tail. The slow tail goes deeper because a\n/// latency distribution is floored below (nothing beats the\n/// fast path) and open above. Deep bands populate only when the\n/// run has enough samples; empty bands are skipped.\nconst BOUNDARY_PCTS: &[f64] = &[\n    0.0,\n    0.0001,\n    0.001,\n    0.01,\n    0.10,\n    0.20,\n    0.30,\n    0.40,\n    0.50,\n    0.60,\n    0.70,\n    0.80,\n    0.90,\n    0.99,\n    0.999,\n    0.9999,\n    0.99999,\n    0.999999,\n    0.9999999,\n    0.99999999,\n    0.999999999,\n    0.9999999999,\n    1.0,\n];\n\n/// zpn-style name per boundary in [`BOUNDARY_PCTS`].\nconst BOUNDARY_NAMES_ZPN: &[&str] = &[\n    \"min\", \"z4\", \"z3\", \"z2\", \"p10\", \"p20\", \"p30\", \"p40\", \"p50\", \"p60\", \"p70\", \"p80\", \"p90\", \"n2\",\n    \"n3\", \"n4\", \"n5\", \"n6\", \"n7\", \"n8\", \"n9\", \"n10\", \"max\",\n];\n\n/// Literal-fraction name per boundary in [`BOUNDARY_PCTS`],\n/// `_`-grouped in threes after the decimal point so deep nines\n/// stay countable at a glance.\nconst BOUNDARY_NAMES_FRAC: &[&str] = &[\n    \"min\",\n    \"0.000_1\",\n    \"0.001\",\n    \"0.01\",\n    \"0.10\",\n    \"0.20\",\n    \"0.30\",\n    \"0.40\",\n    \"0.50\",\n    \"0.60\",\n    \"0.70\",\n    \"0.80\",\n    \"0.90\",\n    \"0.99\",\n    \"0.999\",\n    \"0.999_9\",\n    \"0.999_99\",\n    \"0.999_999\",\n    \"0.999_999_9\",\n    \"0.999_999_99\",\n    \"0.999_999_999\",\n    \"0.999_999_999_9\",\n    \"max\",\n];",
  "new_string": "/// Fast-tail depth: the lowest tail boundary is `z{Z_DEPTH}`.\nconst Z_DEPTH: i32 = 4;\n/// Slow-tail depth: the highest tail boundary is `n{N_DEPTH}`.\n/// Deeper than [`Z_DEPTH`] because a latency distribution is\n/// floored below (nothing beats the fast path) and open above.\nconst N_DEPTH: i32 = 10;\n\n/// One report band boundary: its CDF fraction plus its rendered\n/// name in each label style.\nstruct Boundary {\n    /// Fraction of samples at or below the boundary (CDF).\n    pct: f64,\n    /// nines/zeros / decile name (`z3`, `p50`, `n4`).\n    zpn: String,\n    /// Literal-fraction name, `_`-grouped (`0.001`, `0.999_9`).\n    frac: String,\n}\n\n/// Group `digits` in threes with `_` so deep nines stay\n/// countable at a glance, e.g. `\"99999\"` → `\"999_99\"`.\nfn group3(digits: &str) -> String {\n    let mut out = String::new();\n    for (i, c) in digits.chars().enumerate() {\n        if i > 0 && i % 3 == 0 {\n            out.push('_');\n        }\n        out.push(c);\n    }\n    out\n}\n\n/// The report's band boundaries, generated from\n/// [`Z_DEPTH`]/[`N_DEPTH`]: familiar deciles in the body,\n/// nines/zeros notation in both tails.\n///\n/// - `nK`/`zK` mark the boundary with a fraction 10^-K of\n///   samples above (n) or below (z): n2 ≡ p99, n3 ≡ p99.9, …\n///   and z2 ≡ p1, z3 ≡ p0.1, z4. \"K nines\" is standard\n///   engineering shorthand for proportions near one\n///   (nines = -log10(1-x)); zK is this project's mirror of it\n///   for the fast tail.\n/// - Fractions and names come from one structural description\n///   (the K ranges + deciles), so the label styles can never\n///   drift apart; deepening a tail is a one-constant change.\n/// - Deep bands populate only when the run has enough samples;\n///   empty bands are skipped in the output.\nfn boundaries() -> Vec<Boundary> {\n    let mut v = vec![Boundary {\n        pct: 0.0,\n        zpn: \"min\".to_string(),\n        frac: \"min\".to_string(),\n    }];\n    for k in (2..=Z_DEPTH).rev() {\n        v.push(Boundary {\n            pct: 10f64.powi(-k),\n            zpn: format!(\"z{k}\"),\n            frac: format!(\"0.{}\", group3(&(\"0\".repeat(k as usize - 1) + \"1\"))),\n        });\n    }\n    for d in 1..=9 {\n        v.push(Boundary {\n            pct: d as f64 / 10.0,\n            zpn: format!(\"p{d}0\"),\n            frac: format!(\"0.{d}0\"),\n        });\n    }\n    for k in 2..=N_DEPTH {\n        v.push(Boundary {\n            pct: 1.0 - 10f64.powi(-k),\n            zpn: format!(\"n{k}\"),\n            frac: format!(\"0.{}\", group3(&\"9\".repeat(k as usize))),\n        });\n    }\n    v.push(Boundary {\n        pct: 1.0,\n        zpn: \"max\".to_string(),\n        frac: \"max\".to_string(),\n    });\n    v\n}"
}
```

  "old_string": "    // Row label for the band ending at boundary `i + 1`, per the\n    // selected style. min/max carry no fraction, so `both` prints\n    // them bare; elsewhere `both` pads zpn to its 3-char max so\n    // the fraction column aligns.\n    let band_label = |i: usize| -> String {\n        let zpn = BOUNDARY_NAMES_ZPN[i + 1];\n        let frac = BOUNDARY_NAMES_FRAC[i + 1];\n        match cfg.band_labels {\n            BandLabels::Zpn => zpn.to_string(),\n            BandLabels::Frac => frac.to_string(),\n            BandLabels::Both if zpn == frac => zpn.to_string(),\n            BandLabels::Both => format!(\"{zpn:<3} {frac}\"),\n        }\n    };\n    // Trimmed-stat row prefix labels, matching the band-label\n    // style so `n2` never appears in a frac-only table.\n    let trim_label = match cfg.band_labels {\n        BandLabels::Frac => \"min..0.99\",\n        BandLabels::Zpn | BandLabels::Both => \"min..n2\",\n    };\n    let mean_trim_label = format!(\"mean {trim_label}\");\n    let stdev_trim_label = format!(\"stdev {trim_label}\");\n\n    // Trim anchor: bands at or above the p99 boundary are the\n    // \"tail\" — excluded from the trimmed min..n2 stats no matter\n    // how many finer tail bands subdivide them.\n    #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n    // OK: 0.99 is a literal member of BOUNDARY_PCTS\n    let trim_bands = BOUNDARY_PCTS.iter().position(|&b| b == 0.99).unwrap();\n\n    let n_bands = BOUNDARY_PCTS.len() - 1;",
  "new_string": "    let bounds = boundaries();\n\n    // Row label for the band ending at boundary `i + 1`, per the\n    // selected style. min/max carry no fraction, so `both` prints\n    // them bare; elsewhere `both` pads zpn to its 3-char max so\n    // the fraction column aligns.\n    let band_label = |i: usize| -> String {\n        let b = &bounds[i + 1];\n        match cfg.band_labels {\n            BandLabels::Zpn => b.zpn.clone(),\n            BandLabels::Frac => b.frac.clone(),\n            BandLabels::Both if b.zpn == b.frac => b.zpn.clone(),\n            BandLabels::Both => format!(\"{:<3} {}\", b.zpn, b.frac),\n        }\n    };\n    // Trimmed-stat row prefix labels, matching the band-label\n    // style so `n2` never appears in a frac-only table.\n    let trim_label = match cfg.band_labels {\n        BandLabels::Frac => \"min..0.99\",\n        BandLabels::Zpn | BandLabels::Both => \"min..n2\",\n    };\n    let mean_trim_label = format!(\"mean {trim_label}\");\n    let stdev_trim_label = format!(\"stdev {trim_label}\");\n\n    // Trim anchor: bands at or above the n2 (p99) boundary are\n    // the \"tail\" — excluded from the trimmed min..n2 stats no\n    // matter how many finer tail bands subdivide them.\n    #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n    // OK: boundaries() always emits n2 (N_DEPTH >= 2)\n    let trim_bands = bounds.iter().position(|b| b.zpn == \"n2\").unwrap();\n\n    let n_bands = bounds.len() - 1;"

> AGENT

That `n9` row alone at 3.18 ms with count 1 is the money shot — the whole tail story in one screen. And yes, `3,184,639 ns` is exactly where raw nanoseconds stop being kind to the reader.

Unit scaling has one design tension worth settling when we build it: **per-value auto-scaling breaks vertical comparability** — if `n5` reads `5,478 ns` and `n6` reads `54.4 us`, you can't eyeball ratios down a column anymore. The shape that preserves both: scale **per row** (bands are monotonic, so each row's first/last/mean share a magnitude — the row picks ns/us/ms once), or a `--units ns|auto` flag so saved outputs that scripts parse can stay in fixed ns. It also interacts strongly with `-3` (`--decimals`): `3 ms` with zero decimals destroys information that `3.18 ms` keeps, so units really want decimals landed first. That ordering falls out naturally: `-2` ps recording, `-3` decimals, then units as a possible `-4` grown onto this cycle's ladder (it's squarely in the "report options" theme) — or a ranked todo for after, your call at that point.

For now I'll add it as a todo so it's captured:

**Proposed entry** (slotting after config in rank): "Unit scaling in report columns (`us`/`ms`) — per-row auto-scale to preserve column comparability, or `--units ns|auto`; needs `--decimals` landed first (`3.18 ms` vs `3 ms`); candidate `-4` for the report-options cycle."

And on `-1`: does "kinda pretty" plus your run count as review passed — shall I add that todo, flip the marker to `(done)`, and bring the commit description?

> AGENT

All set. Proposed description:

**Title:** `feat: report option --band-labels`

**Body:**
```
Band-label style option, first of the report-options cycle:
--band-labels zpn|frac|both (default both) selects nines/zeros
names, literal _-grouped fractions, or both side by side; the
header metadata records labels=<style> so saved outputs are
self-describing. Labels and boundary fractions are generated
from one structural description (Z_DEPTH/N_DEPTH + deciles) so
the styles can never drift apart.

- src/harness.rs
  - BandLabels enum (Zpn/Frac/Both + as_str); RunCfg gains
    band_labels
  - boundaries() generator (Boundary, group3, Z_DEPTH=4,
    N_DEPTH=10) replaces the three parallel literal arrays
  - print_report: takes &RunCfg (was &Overhead); labels= in
    header; per-style row labels; style-aware trimmed labels
    (min..n2 / min..0.99); trim anchor found by name, not
    float equality
  - test pinning the generated ladder (zpn + frac names,
    endpoints, monotonicity, n2 anchor)
- src/main.rs: --band-labels flag (ValueEnum, default both)
- src/benches/ (16 files): print_report call passes cfg
- README.md: flag docs + full boundary-ladder mapping table
  (pinned by the unit test)
- notes/todo.md: ladder -1 marked done; new unit-scaling todo
  (ranked #2)
- Cargo.toml, Cargo.lock: 0.14.0-0 -> 0.14.0-1
```

Approve and I'll `vc-x1 push main`.

1	//! Shared band-table renderer for tick-valued histograms.
2	//!
3	//! Both `TProbe` (fast path, direct-histogram) and `TProbe2`
4	//! (scope API, records → drain) store hardware tick deltas and
5	//! want the same band-table output shape — min/p1/…/p99/max
6	//! rows with first/last/range/count/mean columns, plus summary
7	//! lines for mean, stdev, mean min-p99, stdev min-p99. This
8	//! module provides a single implementation both can call into.
9	//!
10	//! Display unit is chosen by `as_ticks`: `false` converts stored
11	//! tick values to nanoseconds via [`crate::ticks::ticks_per_ns`];
12	//! `true` shows raw ticks.
13	
14	use hdrhistogram::Histogram;
15	
16	use crate::harness::{fmt_commas, fmt_commas_f64};
17	use crate::ticks;
18	
19	const BOUNDARY_PCTS: &[f64] = &[
20	    0.0, 0.01, 0.10, 0.20, 0.30, 0.40, 0.50, 0.60, 0.70, 0.80, 0.90, 0.99, 1.0,
21	];
22	const BOUNDARY_NAMES: &[&str] = &[
23	    "min", "p1", "p10", "p20", "p30", "p40", "p50", "p60", "p70", "p80", "p90", "p99", "max",
24	];
25	
26	/// Render a band-table report for `hist`, interpreting stored
27	/// values as hardware ticks. `kind` is the header label
28	/// (`"tprobe"`, `"tprobe2"`, …) and `name` is the probe's name.
29	/// `as_ticks=false` converts to ns; `true` keeps raw ticks.
30	pub(crate) fn render(kind: &str, name: &str, hist: &Histogram<u64>, as_ticks: bool) {
31	    let sample_count = hist.len();
32	    println!("  {kind}: {name} [count={}]", fmt_commas(sample_count));
33	    if sample_count == 0 {
34	        println!();
35	        return;
36	    }
37	
38	    let unit = if as_ticks { "tk" } else { "ns" };
39	    let tpn = ticks::ticks_per_ns();
40	    let conv = |v: u64| -> f64 { if as_ticks { v as f64 } else { v as f64 / tpn } };
41	    let conv_f = |v: f64| -> f64 { if as_ticks { v } else { v / tpn } };
42	
43	    let n_bands = BOUNDARY_PCTS.len() - 1;
44	    let mut band_first = vec![u64::MAX; n_bands];
45	    let mut band_last = vec![0u64; n_bands];
46	    let mut band_count = vec![0u64; n_bands];
47	    let mut band_sum = vec![0u128; n_bands];
48	
49	    let mut cumulative = 0u64;
50	    for iv in hist.iter_recorded() {
51	        let value = iv.value_iterated_to();
52	        let count = iv.count_at_value();
53	        let mid_rank = (cumulative as f64 + count as f64 / 2.0) / sample_count as f64;
54	        let idx = BOUNDARY_PCTS[1..]
55	            .iter()
56	            .position(|&b| mid_rank < b)
57	            .unwrap_or(n_bands - 1);
58	        band_first[idx] = band_first[idx].min(value);
59	        band_last[idx] = band_last[idx].max(value);
60	        band_count[idx] += count;
61	        band_sum[idx] += value as u128 * count as u128;
62	        cumulative += count;
63	    }
64	
65	    struct BandRow {
66	        label: String,
67	        first: String,
68	        last: String,
69	        range: String,
70	        count: String,
71	        mean: String,
72	    }
73	
74	    let mut rows: Vec<BandRow> = Vec::new();
75	    for i in 0..n_bands {
76	        if band_count[i] == 0 {
77	            continue;
78	        }
79	        let mean_val = band_sum[i] as f64 / band_count[i] as f64;
80	        let range_raw = band_last[i] - band_first[i] + 1;
81	        rows.push(BandRow {
82	            label: format!("{}-{}", BOUNDARY_NAMES[i], BOUNDARY_NAMES[i + 1]),
83	            first: fmt_commas_f64(conv(band_first[i]), 0),
84	            last: fmt_commas_f64(conv(band_last[i]), 0),
85	            range: fmt_commas_f64(conv(range_raw), 0),
86	            count: fmt_commas(band_count[i]),
87	            mean: fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(mean_val), 0),
88	        });
89	    }
90	
91	    let label_w = rows
92	        .iter()
93	        .map(|r| r.label.len())
94	        .max()
95	        .unwrap_or(0)
96	        .max("stdev min-p99".len());
97	    let first_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.first.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
98	    let last_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.last.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
99	    let range_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.range.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
100	    let count_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.count.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
101	    let mean_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.mean.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
102	
103	    const INDENT: &str = "    ";
104	    const GAP: &str = "    ";
105	
106	    let first_col = INDENT.len() + label_w + 1 + first_w;
107	    let unit_len = 1 + unit.len();
108	    let last_gap = unit_len + GAP.len() + last_w;
109	    let range_gap = unit_len + GAP.len() + range_w;
110	    let count_gap = unit_len + GAP.len() + count_w;
111	    let mean_gap = GAP.len() + mean_w;
112	    println!(
113	        "{:>first_col$}{:>last_gap$}{:>range_gap$}{:>count_gap$}{:>mean_gap$}",
114	        "first", "last", "range", "count", "mean",
115	    );
116	
117	    for r in &rows {
118	        println!(
119	            "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>first_w$} {unit}{GAP}{:>last_w$} {unit}{GAP}{:>range_w$} {unit}{GAP}{:>count_w$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}",
120	            r.label, r.first, r.last, r.range, r.count, r.mean,
121	        );
122	    }
123	
124	    let hist_mean = hist.mean();
125	    let skip = first_w
126	        + unit_len
127	        + GAP.len()
128	        + last_w
129	        + unit_len
130	        + GAP.len()
131	        + range_w
132	        + unit_len
133	        + GAP.len()
134	        + count_w;
135	    println!(
136	        "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}",
137	        "mean",
138	        "",
139	        fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(hist_mean), 0),
140	    );
141	    println!(
142	        "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}",
143	        "stdev",
144	        "",
145	        fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(hist.stdev()), 0),
146	    );
147	
148	    let trim_count: u64 = band_count[..n_bands - 1].iter().sum();
149	    if trim_count > 0 {
150	        let trim_sum: u128 = band_sum[..n_bands - 1].iter().sum();
151	        let trim_mean = trim_sum as f64 / trim_count as f64;
152	
153	        let mut trim_var_sum = 0.0f64;
154	        let mut trim_var_count = 0u64;
155	        let mut cum = 0u64;
156	        for iv in hist.iter_recorded() {
157	            let value = iv.value_iterated_to();
158	            let count = iv.count_at_value();
159	            let mid_rank = (cum as f64 + count as f64 / 2.0) / sample_count as f64;
160	            let idx = BOUNDARY_PCTS[1..]
161	                .iter()
162	                .position(|&b| mid_rank < b)
163	                .unwrap_or(n_bands - 1);
164	            if idx < n_bands - 1 {
165	                let diff = value as f64 - trim_mean;
166	                trim_var_sum += diff * diff * count as f64;
167	                trim_var_count += count;
168	            }
169	            cum += count;
170	        }
171	        let trim_stdev = if trim_var_count > 1 {
172	            (trim_var_sum / trim_var_count as f64).sqrt()
173	        } else {
174	            0.0
175	        };
176	
177	        println!(
178	            "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}",
179	            "mean min-p99",
180	            "",
181	            fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(trim_mean), 0),
182	        );
183	        println!(
184	            "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}",
185	            "stdev min-p99",
186	            "",
187	            fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(trim_stdev), 0),
188	        );
189	    }
190	    println!();
191	}
192	
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/bands.rs",
  "content": "//! Report band boundaries and label styles: the single source of\n//! truth for the min/z/p/n/max ladder that\n//! [`crate::harness::print_report`] renders, documented by the\n//! README's boundary-ladder table (pinned by this module's tests).\n//!\n//! - Familiar deciles in the body; nines/zeros tails generated\n//!   from [`Z_DEPTH`]/[`N_DEPTH`]. Fractions and names come from\n//!   one structural description, so the label styles can never\n//!   drift apart and deepening a tail is a one-constant change.\n//! - `band_table.rs` and `probe.rs` still carry their own older\n//!   percentile ladders; unifying them onto this module is part\n//!   of the probe-based-harness conversion todo.\n\n/// Fast-tail depth: the lowest tail boundary is `z{Z_DEPTH}`.\nconst Z_DEPTH: i32 = 4;\n/// Slow-tail depth: the highest tail boundary is `n{N_DEPTH}`.\n/// Deeper than [`Z_DEPTH`] because a latency distribution is\n/// floored below (nothing beats the fast path) and open above.\nconst N_DEPTH: i32 = 10;\n\n/// Band-label style for the report's histogram rows, selected by\n/// the `--band-labels` CLI flag.\n///\n/// - `Zpn` — nines/zeros + decile names (`z3`, `p50`, `n4`).\n/// - `Frac` — literal boundary fractions with `_` grouping\n///   (`0.001`, `0.50`, `0.999_9`).\n/// - `Both` — zpn and fraction side by side (`n4  0.999_9`);\n///   the default: the juxtaposition teaches the zpn vocabulary.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, clap::ValueEnum)]\npub enum BandLabels {\n    Zpn,\n    Frac,\n    Both,\n}\n\nimpl BandLabels {\n    /// Lowercase name used on the CLI and in the report header's\n    /// `labels=` metadata.\n    pub fn as_str(self) -> &'static str {\n        match self {\n            BandLabels::Zpn => \"zpn\",\n            BandLabels::Frac => \"frac\",\n            BandLabels::Both => \"both\",\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Trimmed-stat row range label matching the style, so `n2`\n    /// never appears in a frac-only table.\n    pub fn trim_label(self) -> &'static str {\n        match self {\n            BandLabels::Frac => \"min..0.99\",\n            BandLabels::Zpn | BandLabels::Both => \"min..n2\",\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// One report band boundary: its CDF fraction plus its rendered\n/// name in each label style.\npub struct Boundary {\n    /// Fraction of samples at or below the boundary (CDF).\n    pub pct: f64,\n    /// nines/zeros / decile name (`z3`, `p50`, `n4`).\n    pub zpn: String,\n    /// Literal-fraction name, `_`-grouped (`0.001`, `0.999_9`).\n    pub frac: String,\n}\n\nimpl Boundary {\n    /// Row label for this boundary in the given style. min/max\n    /// carry no fraction, so `both` prints them bare; elsewhere\n    /// `both` pads zpn to its 3-char max so the fraction column\n    /// aligns.\n    pub fn label(&self, style: BandLabels) -> String {\n        match style {\n            BandLabels::Zpn => self.zpn.clone(),\n            BandLabels::Frac => self.frac.clone(),\n            BandLabels::Both if self.zpn == self.frac => self.zpn.clone(),\n            BandLabels::Both => format!(\"{:<3} {}\", self.zpn, self.frac),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Group `digits` in threes with `_` so deep nines stay\n/// countable at a glance, e.g. `\"99999\"` → `\"999_99\"`.\nfn group3(digits: &str) -> String {\n    let mut out = String::new();\n    for (i, c) in digits.chars().enumerate() {\n        if i > 0 && i % 3 == 0 {\n            out.push('_');\n        }\n        out.push(c);\n    }\n    out\n}\n\n/// The report's band boundaries, generated from\n/// [`Z_DEPTH`]/[`N_DEPTH`]: familiar deciles in the body,\n/// nines/zeros notation in both tails.\n///\n/// - `nK`/`zK` mark the boundary with a fraction 10^-K of\n///   samples above (n) or below (z): n2 ≡ p99, n3 ≡ p99.9, …\n///   and z2 ≡ p1, z3 ≡ p0.1, z4. \"K nines\" is standard\n///   engineering shorthand for proportions near one\n///   (nines = -log10(1-x)); zK is this project's mirror of it\n///   for the fast tail.\n/// - Deep bands populate only when the run has enough samples;\n///   empty bands are skipped in the output.\npub fn boundaries() -> Vec<Boundary> {\n    let mut v = vec![Boundary {\n        pct: 0.0,\n        zpn: \"min\".to_string(),\n        frac: \"min\".to_string(),\n    }];\n    for k in (2..=Z_DEPTH).rev() {\n        v.push(Boundary {\n            pct: 10f64.powi(-k),\n            zpn: format!(\"z{k}\"),\n            frac: format!(\"0.{}\", group3(&(\"0\".repeat(k as usize - 1) + \"1\"))),\n        });\n    }\n    for d in 1..=9 {\n        v.push(Boundary {\n            pct: d as f64 / 10.0,\n            zpn: format!(\"p{d}0\"),\n            frac: format!(\"0.{d}0\"),\n        });\n    }\n    for k in 2..=N_DEPTH {\n        v.push(Boundary {\n            pct: 1.0 - 10f64.powi(-k),\n            zpn: format!(\"n{k}\"),\n            frac: format!(\"0.{}\", group3(&\"9\".repeat(k as usize))),\n        });\n    }\n    v.push(Boundary {\n        pct: 1.0,\n        zpn: \"max\".to_string(),\n        frac: \"max\".to_string(),\n    });\n    v\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn generated_boundaries_match_documented_ladder() {\n        let b = boundaries();\n        let zpn: Vec<&str> = b.iter().map(|x| x.zpn.as_str()).collect();\n        assert_eq!(\n            zpn,\n            [\n                \"min\", \"z4\", \"z3\", \"z2\", \"p10\", \"p20\", \"p30\", \"p40\", \"p50\", \"p60\", \"p70\", \"p80\",\n                \"p90\", \"n2\", \"n3\", \"n4\", \"n5\", \"n6\", \"n7\", \"n8\", \"n9\", \"n10\", \"max\",\n            ]\n        );\n        let frac: Vec<&str> = b.iter().map(|x| x.frac.as_str()).collect();\n        assert_eq!(\n            frac,\n            [\n                \"min\",\n                \"0.000_1\",\n                \"0.001\",\n                \"0.01\",\n                \"0.10\",\n                \"0.20\",\n                \"0.30\",\n                \"0.40\",\n                \"0.50\",\n                \"0.60\",\n                \"0.70\",\n                \"0.80\",\n                \"0.90\",\n                \"0.99\",\n                \"0.999\",\n                \"0.999_9\",\n                \"0.999_99\",\n                \"0.999_999\",\n                \"0.999_999_9\",\n                \"0.999_999_99\",\n                \"0.999_999_999\",\n                \"0.999_999_999_9\",\n                \"max\",\n            ]\n        );\n        // Endpoints exact; interior strictly increasing; the n2\n        // trim anchor lands within a ulp of 0.99.\n        assert_eq!(b[0].pct, 0.0);\n        assert_eq!(b[b.len() - 1].pct, 1.0);\n        assert!(b.windows(2).all(|w| w[0].pct < w[1].pct));\n        let n2 = &b[13];\n        assert!((n2.pct - 0.99).abs() < 1e-12, \"n2 pct: {}\", n2.pct);\n    }\n}\n"
}

  "old_string": "/// `CLOCK_BOOTTIME` minus `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` elapsed divergence\n/// (seconds) at or above which [`warn_invalid`] reports that the\n/// system suspended during the run.\nconst SUSPEND_WARN_S: f64 = 1.0;\n\n/// Fast-tail depth: the lowest tail boundary is `z{Z_DEPTH}`.\nconst Z_DEPTH: i32 = 4;\n/// Slow-tail depth: the highest tail boundary is `n{N_DEPTH}`.\n/// Deeper than [`Z_DEPTH`] because a latency distribution is\n/// floored below (nothing beats the fast path) and open above.\nconst N_DEPTH: i32 = 10;\n\n/// One report band boundary: its CDF fraction plus its rendered\n/// name in each label style.\nstruct Boundary {\n    /// Fraction of samples at or below the boundary (CDF).\n    pct: f64,\n    /// nines/zeros / decile name (`z3`, `p50`, `n4`).\n    zpn: String,\n    /// Literal-fraction name, `_`-grouped (`0.001`, `0.999_9`).\n    frac: String,\n}\n\n/// Group `digits` in threes with `_` so deep nines stay\n/// countable at a glance, e.g. `\"99999\"` → `\"999_99\"`.\nfn group3(digits: &str) -> String {\n    let mut out = String::new();\n    for (i, c) in digits.chars().enumerate() {\n        if i > 0 && i % 3 == 0 {\n            out.push('_');\n        }\n        out.push(c);\n    }\n    out\n}\n\n/// The report's band boundaries, generated from\n/// [`Z_DEPTH`]/[`N_DEPTH`]: familiar deciles in the body,\n/// nines/zeros notation in both tails.\n///\n/// - `nK`/`zK` mark the boundary with a fraction 10^-K of\n///   samples above (n) or below (z): n2 ≡ p99, n3 ≡ p99.9, …\n///   and z2 ≡ p1, z3 ≡ p0.1, z4. \"K nines\" is standard\n///   engineering shorthand for proportions near one\n///   (nines = -log10(1-x)); zK is this project's mirror of it\n///   for the fast tail.\n/// - Fractions and names come from one structural description\n///   (the K ranges + deciles), so the label styles can never\n///   drift apart; deepening a tail is a one-constant change.\n/// - Deep bands populate only when the run has enough samples;\n///   empty bands are skipped in the output.\nfn boundaries() -> Vec<Boundary> {\n    let mut v = vec![Boundary {\n        pct: 0.0,\n        zpn: \"min\".to_string(),\n        frac: \"min\".to_string(),\n    }];\n    for k in (2..=Z_DEPTH).rev() {\n        v.push(Boundary {\n            pct: 10f64.powi(-k),\n            zpn: format!(\"z{k}\"),\n            frac: format!(\"0.{}\", group3(&(\"0\".repeat(k as usize - 1) + \"1\"))),\n        });\n    }\n    for d in 1..=9 {\n        v.push(Boundary {\n            pct: d as f64 / 10.0,\n            zpn: format!(\"p{d}0\"),\n            frac: format!(\"0.{d}0\"),\n        });\n    }\n    for k in 2..=N_DEPTH {\n        v.push(Boundary {\n            pct: 1.0 - 10f64.powi(-k),\n            zpn: format!(\"n{k}\"),\n            frac: format!(\"0.{}\", group3(&\"9\".repeat(k as usize))),\n        });\n    }\n    v.push(Boundary {\n        pct: 1.0,\n        zpn: \"max\".to_string(),\n        frac: \"max\".to_string(),\n    });\n    v\n}\n\n/// Band-label style for the report's histogram rows, selected by\n/// the `--band-labels` CLI flag.\n///\n/// - `Zpn` — nines/zeros + decile names (`z3`, `p50`, `n4`).\n/// - `Frac` — literal boundary fractions with `_` grouping\n///   (`0.001`, `0.50`, `0.999_9`).\n/// - `Both` — zpn and fraction side by side (`n4  0.999_9`);\n///   the default: the juxtaposition teaches the zpn vocabulary.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, clap::ValueEnum)]\npub enum BandLabels {\n    Zpn,\n    Frac,\n    Both,\n}\n\nimpl BandLabels {\n    /// Lowercase name used on the CLI and in the report header's\n    /// `labels=` metadata.\n    pub fn as_str(self) -> &'static str {\n        match self {\n            BandLabels::Zpn => \"zpn\",\n            BandLabels::Frac => \"frac\",\n            BandLabels::Both => \"both\",\n        }\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// `CLOCK_BOOTTIME` minus `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` elapsed divergence\n/// (seconds) at or above which [`warn_invalid`] reports that the\n/// system suspended during the run.\nconst SUSPEND_WARN_S: f64 = 1.0;"
}
```

in a new bands module from one structural description
(Z_DEPTH/N_DEPTH + deciles) so they can never drift apart.

  future home for band_table.rs / probe.rs ladder unification.

  &RunCfg (was &Overhead), records labels= in the header,

- src/benches/ (16 files): print_report call passes cfg
- README.md: flag docs + full boundary-ladder mapping table,
  pointing at src/bands.rs as the source of truth
- notes/todo.md: ladder -1 marked done; new unit-scaling todo
  (ranked #2)
- Cargo.toml, Cargo.lock: 0.14.0-0 -> 0.14.0-1
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/harness.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Histogram value bounds: 1 ns to 60 s at 3 sig figs. The high\n/// bound is a sane-world ceiling for one recorded sample, not a\n/// technical limit — [`record_sample`] clamps above it and\n/// [`warn_invalid`] flags the run.\nconst HIST_LOW_NS: u64 = 1;\nconst HIST_HIGH_NS: u64 = 60_000_000_000;",
  "new_string": "/// Histogram value bounds: 1 ps to 60 s at 3 sig figs. Values\n/// are recorded in **picoseconds** — the timer reads integer ns,\n/// but dividing a sample by `inner` in ps keeps the true sub-ns\n/// per-call precision that ns recording truncated (a 4.7 ns call\n/// no longer rounds to 5). The high bound is a sane-world\n/// ceiling for one recorded sample, not a technical limit —\n/// [`record_sample`] clamps above it and [`warn_invalid`] flags\n/// the run.\nconst HIST_LOW_PS: u64 = 1;\nconst HIST_HIGH_PS: u64 = 60_000_000_000_000;\n\n/// Picoseconds per nanosecond: recorded values are ps, display\n/// is ns.\nconst PS_PER_NS: f64 = 1000.0;"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/harness.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Fresh histogram over `[HIST_LOW_NS, HIST_HIGH_NS]` at 3 sig\n/// figs, resize disabled — out-of-range samples clamp (see\n/// [`record_sample`]) rather than grow the histogram.\nfn new_hist() -> Histogram<u64> {\n    Histogram::<u64>::new_with_bounds(HIST_LOW_NS, HIST_HIGH_NS, 3).unwrap() // OK: constant bounds\n}\n\n/// Time one sample (`inner` back-to-back calls), divide down to a\n/// per-call value, and record it, clamping at the histogram\n/// bounds — a suspend-inflated or wedged sample must not panic a\n/// long run ([`warn_invalid`] flags it instead).\nfn record_sample<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B, inner: u64, hist: &mut Histogram<u64>) {\n    let start = minstant::Instant::now();\n    for _ in 0..inner {\n        black_box(bench.step());\n    }\n    let elapsed_ns = start.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;\n    hist.saturating_record(round_elapsed(elapsed_ns, inner));\n}\n\nfn round_elapsed(elapsed_ns: u64, inner: u64) -> u64 {\n    (elapsed_ns + inner / 2) / inner\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Fresh histogram over `[HIST_LOW_PS, HIST_HIGH_PS]` at 3 sig\n/// figs, resize disabled — out-of-range samples clamp (see\n/// [`record_sample`]) rather than grow the histogram.\nfn new_hist() -> Histogram<u64> {\n    Histogram::<u64>::new_with_bounds(HIST_LOW_PS, HIST_HIGH_PS, 3).unwrap() // OK: constant bounds\n}\n\n/// Time one sample (`inner` back-to-back calls), divide down to a\n/// per-call value in **picoseconds**, and record it, clamping at\n/// the histogram bounds — a suspend-inflated or wedged sample\n/// must not panic a long run ([`warn_invalid`] flags it instead).\nfn record_sample<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B, inner: u64, hist: &mut Histogram<u64>) {\n    let start = minstant::Instant::now();\n    for _ in 0..inner {\n        black_box(bench.step());\n    }\n    let elapsed_ps = start.elapsed().as_nanos().saturating_mul(1000);\n    hist.saturating_record(round_elapsed_ps(elapsed_ps, inner));\n}\n\n/// Per-call value: `elapsed_ps / inner`, rounded to nearest, in\n/// u128 so an hours-long suspend-inflated sample can't overflow\n/// the ×1000 ns→ps conversion; the cast clamps at u64::MAX and\n/// `saturating_record` clamps again at the histogram bound.\nfn round_elapsed_ps(elapsed_ps: u128, inner: u64) -> u64 {\n    let inner = inner as u128;\n    ((elapsed_ps + inner / 2) / inner).min(u64::MAX as u128) as u64\n}"
}

> AGENT

`-2` complete, and the before/after comparison shows exactly the promised effect. Work summary and evidence:

**Before (0.14.0-1)** vs **after (0.14.0-2)** on `zcr-spin-1t -d 3` (inner ≈ 30, where ns-truncation bites):

- Trimmed `mean min..n2` adjusted: **5 ns → 4 ns** — the truth was ~4.x all along; ns recording rounded each per-call sample up to 5 before averaging.
- Body bands now have sub-ns width: rows show `range 0 ns` (e.g. `p60` first=last=5 ns) because samples like 4.727 and 4.933 are now distinct recorded values instead of both piling onto integer 5 — the display just can't show the spread until `--decimals` lands next step. That `0 ns` is a known intermediate wart, cured by `-3`.
- Tail rows unchanged in character (they're µs-scale; ps adds nothing there — as predicted, `inner=1` values gain no precision).

Implementation: bounds are now `HIST_LOW_PS`/`HIST_HIGH_PS` (1 ps – 60 s, same 60 s ceiling), `record_sample` converts ns→ps in `u128` and `round_elapsed_ps` divides by `inner` rounding-to-nearest with saturation (an hours-long suspend sample can't overflow), all display paths convert ps→ns, `warn_invalid` clamps against the ps bound. Two tests added: the 156 ns/33 = 4,727 ps precision case, and overflow saturation. fmt/clippy/test clean (16 tests), installed. Both comparison files saved in the scratchpad.

Proposed description:

**Title:** `feat: report picosecond recording`

**Body:**
```
Record histogram values in picoseconds instead of nanoseconds:
the timer reads integer ns, but dividing a sample by inner in
ps keeps the true sub-ns per-call precision that ns recording
truncated (a 4.727 ns/call sample recorded as 5 ns before) -
visible already as the trimmed zcr-spin-1t adjusted mean
dropping 5 -> 4 ns. Display stays integer ns until --decimals
lands next step; sub-ns band ranges show as 0 ns until then.

- src/harness.rs
  - HIST_LOW_PS / HIST_HIGH_PS bounds (1 ps .. 60 s); PS_PER_NS
  - record_sample: ns->ps in u128; round_elapsed_ps divides by
    inner rounding to nearest, saturating on absurd inputs
  - print_report: first/last/range/mean/stdev and trimmed
    stats convert ps->ns for display
  - warn_invalid: clamp check against HIST_HIGH_PS
  - tests: clamp in ps; round_elapsed_ps precision + saturation
- Cargo.toml, Cargo.lock: 0.14.0-1 -> 0.14.0-2
```

Review at your leisure — on approval I'll flip the ladder marker and push.

> AGENT

Updated description for `-2`:

**Title:** `feat: report picosecond recording`

**Body:**
```
Record histogram values in picoseconds instead of nanoseconds:
the timer reads integer ns, but dividing a sample by inner in
ps keeps the true sub-ns per-call precision that ns recording
truncated. Time columns default to 1 decimal so the gained
precision is visible - zcr-spin-1t's body now resolves into
4.8/5.2/5.5/5.9 ns rows that were two integer bins before, and
its trimmed adjusted mean drops 5 -> 4.6 ns (ns-rounding
inflation removed). --decimals lands next step to control it.

- src/harness.rs
  - HIST_LOW_PS / HIST_HIGH_PS bounds (1 ps .. 60 s); PS_PER_NS
  - record_sample: ns->ps in u128; round_elapsed_ps divides by
    inner rounding to nearest, saturating on absurd inputs
  - print_report: first/last/range/mean/stdev and trimmed
    stats convert ps->ns; TIME_DECIMALS = 1 on time columns
  - warn_invalid: clamp check against HIST_HIGH_PS
  - tests: clamp in ps; round_elapsed_ps precision + saturation
- notes/todo.md: -2/-3 ladder scope updated (default decimal
  moved into -2)
- Cargo.toml, Cargo.lock: 0.14.0-1 -> 0.14.0-2
```

Approve and I'll flip the marker to `(done)` and push.

`-3` complete and verified: `--decimals 0` restores integers, `--decimals 3` shows the full ps floor (`9.823 ns`), `--decimals 5` is rejected by clap's range check, and the default stays 1. fmt/clippy/test clean, installed.

Proposed description:

**Title:** `feat: report option --decimals`

**Body:**
```
--decimals N (0-3, default 1) controls the decimal digits on
the report's time columns, completing the control for the
1-digit default that ps recording introduced: 0 restores
integer ns, 3 is the recording floor - more digits would be
artifacts. clap rejects values above 3.

- src/harness.rs: RunCfg gains decimals; print_report time
  columns use cfg.decimals (TIME_DECIMALS const removed)
- src/main.rs: --decimals flag (u8, range 0..=3,
  default_value_t = 1)
- README.md: flag docs (notes ps-internal recording)
- Cargo.toml, Cargo.lock: 0.14.0-2 -> 0.14.0-3
```

Approve and I'll flip the marker and push — then all that remains for the cycle is the `0.14.0` close-out (bookkeeping: Done entry, In Progress → chores narrative with as-built ladder, README check).

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/notes/chores/chores-04.md",
  "old_string": "## feat: report options + ps recording\n\nCommits:",
  "new_string": "## feat: report options + ps recording\n\nCommits:\n\nBand-label style, decimal display, and picosecond recording —\nevaluated as CLI options ahead of the config file (options\nfirst so we could see how they look; persistence afterwards).\nDecimals needed ps recording to be uniform: mean/adjusted were\nf64 already, but first/last/range were integer-ns recorded\nvalues until the recording unit became ps.\n\n- `--band-labels zpn|frac|both` (default both): labels and\n  boundary fractions generated in the new `src/bands.rs` from\n  one structural description (Z_DEPTH/N_DEPTH + deciles), so\n  the styles can't drift; README's ladder table is pinned by\n  the module's test. Header records `labels=<style>`.\n- Picosecond recording: dividing a sample by `inner` in ps\n  keeps true sub-ns per-call precision — zcr-spin-1t's body\n  resolved into 4.8/5.2/5.5/5.9 ns rows that were two integer\n  bins, and its trimmed adjusted mean dropped 5 → 4.6 ns\n  (ns-rounding inflation removed).\n- `--decimals N` (0–3, default 1) controls time-column digits;\n  0 restores integers, 3 is the recording floor.\n\n### As-built ladder\n\n- 0.14.0-0 `chore: open report options cycle`\n- 0.14.0-1 `feat: report option --band-labels`\n- 0.14.0-2 `feat: report picosecond recording`\n- 0.14.0-3 `feat: report option --decimals`\n- 0.14.0 `feat: report options + ps recording` — close-out\n\nThe -2/-3 order was swapped from the original plan (decimals\nwere to precede ps) so the display default could be designed\nagainst the real post-ps precision; the 1-digit default then\nlanded in -2 itself to make the ps gain visible immediately."
}
```

1	# Versioning
2	
3	How this project versions its commits and the running
4	artifact. The scheme — the cycle step-numbering and the
5	unique-per-commit aim — is generic and shared across projects:
6	this file is copied **verbatim**, with
7	[Recording the version-of-record](#recording-the-version-of-record)
8	covering each medium by conditional rather than per-project
9	edits.
10	
11	## Terms
12	
13	Three names, used as defined here across
14	[AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md),
15	[cycle-protocol.md](cycle-protocol.md), and the notes files:
16	
17	- **version** — the per-commit version (e.g. `0.3.0-5.3.0`),
18	  written with `-` in prose, ladders, chores, todo, and commit
19	  talk; its suffix encodes the cycle phase (see
20	  [Step numbering](#step-numbering)).
21	- **version-of-record** — the authoritative stored copy of the
22	  version, in the project's manifest (see
23	  [Recording the version-of-record](#recording-the-version-of-record));
24	  a built or running artifact derives from it.
25	- **versioning** — the topic: this scheme as a whole.
26	
27	## Recording the version-of-record
28	
29	Where the version-of-record lives, how it's stored and
30	surfaced, and how often it changes — pick the case that fits
31	your medium:
32	
33	- **Manifest** — where the version-of-record is stored:
34	  - if Rust, `Cargo.toml` `[package].version`
35	  - if Python, `pyproject.toml` `[project].version` (or the
36	    committed config it's sourced from)
37	  - otherwise wherever the medium records it (a generic
38	    `version.toml`, a book's frontmatter, …) — add the case as
39	    needed
40	- **Notation** — how the `-` form is stored:
41	  - if the format allows `-` (TOML `version.toml`, `Cargo.toml`),
42	    store it verbatim
43	  - if it bars `-` (PEP 440's local segment, e.g. a Python
44	    project), remap to `+` — `0.3.0-5.3.0` → `0.3.0+5.3.0`: same
45	    version, just the stored spelling
46	- **Reporter** — how a built artifact surfaces the
47	  version-of-record:
48	  - if a CLI app, `<cli-app> -V`
49	  - if a TUI/GUI, add to Help/About or display on the title
50	- **Cadence** — how often to bump: see
51	  [Unique per commit](#unique-per-commit-preference-not-requirement);
52	  this project follows the per-commit preference.
53	
54	## Unique per commit (preference, not requirement)
55	
56	Our general notion is that the version-of-record should change
57	on **every commit**, so a built or running artifact identifies
58	the exact commit it came from.
59	
60	- This is a preference, **not** a hard requirement. A project
61	  following the cycle protocol may bump less often — once per
62	  cycle, only at release, and so on. Record the choice in
63	  [Recording the version-of-record](#recording-the-version-of-record)
64	  if it differs.
65	- It is achievable because the cycle's versions (below) are
66	  **pre-assignable**, unlike the git SHA, which a commit cannot
67	  contain (see the cycle protocol's
68	  [Commits backfill](cycle-protocol.md#commits-backfill)).
69	
70	## Step numbering
71	
72	The cycle (Preparation -> Work -> Close-out; see
73	[cycle-protocol.md](cycle-protocol.md)) encodes each commit's
74	phase in the version suffix — the **final identifier `0` marks
75	a Preparation**:
76	
77	- `X.Y.Z-0` — Preparation
78	- `X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, … — Work commits
79	- `X.Y.Z` — Close-out (bare version, no suffix)
80	
81	**Preparation is optional.** A lightweight cycle — no ladder,
82	no setup commit — skips `-0` and starts at `-1` (its first
83	Work commit). The same holds at every level: a sub-cycle
84	needing no Preparation omits its `.0` (see Nesting). One that
85	grows a Preparation later adds the `0` step without
86	renumbering siblings.
87	
88	Disambiguation:
89	
90	- `-10` — Work commit #10 (final identifier `10`), not a
91	  Preparation.
92	- `-1.0` — Preparation of the `-1` sub-cycle (final
93	  identifier `0`).
94	
95	**Nesting.** Sub-cycles append another level, recursively:
96	
97	- `X.Y.Z-3.0` — Preparation of the `-3` sub-cycle
98	- `X.Y.Z-3.1`, `X.Y.Z-3.2` — its Work
99	- `X.Y.Z-3` — its Close-out
100	- `X.Y.Z-3.1.0` — Preparation of the `-3.1` sub-sub-cycle
101	
102	Bump the version-of-record at the start of each phase, so the
103	active phase is recorded — and, per the preference above,
104	every commit carries a distinct version.
105	
```
