1	# Cycle protocol
2	
3	This protocol uses [Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form). It
4	contains instructions on how a commit cycle is accomplished.
5	
6	The artifact a cycle produces is whatever the bot generates from
7	the conversation — code, prose, an image, a song, a screenplay.
8	The steps below use a Rust crate as the running example (the
9	cargo cycle, `Cargo.toml` versioning); substitute your medium's
10	equivalents — this project's manifest is recorded in
11	[versioning.md](versioning.md).
12	
13	## Cycles
14	
15	A cycle has three phases:
16	
17	- **[Preparation](#preparation)** (`X.Y.Z-0`) — the cycle's
18	  first commit, when it needs setup (a lightweight cycle omits
19	  it and starts at `-1` — see
20	  [versioning.md](versioning.md#step-numbering)). Sets up the
21	  cycle:
22	  - Bump the version-of-record to `X.Y.Z-0` (where it lives
23	    and the suffix scheme are project-specific — see
24	    [versioning.md](versioning.md)).
25	  - Pick up a `## Todo` item (typically the top-ranked,
26	    #1) into `## In Progress` (bold title + succinct problem
27	    statement + plan ladder).
28	  - Open the [chores section](#chores-sections).
29	- **[Work-N](#work-n)** (`X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, …) — the
30	  commits that implement the change. As many as the change
31	  needs; each runs through the
32	  [per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow).
33	- **[Close-out](#close-out)** (bare `X.Y.Z`) — the cycle's
34	  last commit. Bookkeeping only:
35	  - Move the picked-up item to a one-line entry in
36	    `## Done`.
37	  - Move the `## In Progress` block into the
38	    [chores section](#chores-sections).
39	  - Optionally update `notes/README.md` if functionality
40	    changed.
41	
42	A cycle's commits are published to the project remote
43	either incrementally or as one batch at close-out; the
44	result must always be published at close-out. See
45	[Pushing](#pushing).
46	
47	**Sub-cycles.** When a Work commit's scope grows enough to
48	warrant its own ladder, it subdivides — `X.Y.Z-3.0`
49	Preparation, `X.Y.Z-3.1` / `X.Y.Z-3.2` Work, `X.Y.Z-3`
50	Close-out. The same three-phase shape applies recursively
51	at every depth. See [Numbering](#numbering) for the
52	suffix rule and [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders)
53	for the local-ladder mechanics.
54	
55	## Chores sections
56	
57	A **chores section** is a `##` section in
58	`notes/chores/chores-NN.md` recording landed work. In
59	general, every commit that lands on the permanent branch
60	should have a reference to it on a `Commits:` list in a
61	chores file.
62	
63	The phrase **"Open" the chores section** means append a
64	`##` header to the current `notes/chores/chores-NN.md`
65	with the title it records (e.g. `## refactor: foo bar`),
66	followed by an **empty `Commits:`** line. The narrative
67	body stays empty until close-out (see [Close-out](#close-out));
68	the `Commits:` line is backfilled later, once the commit is
69	permanent (see [Commits backfill](#commits-backfill) below).
70	
71	Fuller chores conventions (content rules, header sync,
72	design subsection pattern, `Commits:` formatting) live in
73	AGENTS.md [Chores conventions](../AGENTS.md#chores-conventions).
74	
75	### Commits backfill
76	
77	A chores section's `Commits:` line cites the commit(s) it
78	records, by SHA — but a SHA isn't stable until the commit
79	lands on a **permanent branch** (`main`, or a long-lived
80	release/patch branch that won't be rewritten); a rebase or
81	squash rewrites it on the way. So:
82	
83	- A chores section is **opened with an empty `Commits:`** line.
84	- **Backfill once the commit is on a permanent branch**,
85	  where its SHA is final. A commit can't record its own SHA
86	  (that would change the hash), so the fill always lands one
87	  push later: **each push backfills the `Commits:` of the
88	  commits the previous push made permanent.** On a topic
89	  branch the sections instead wait until the branch lands —
90	  so no SHA is ever written that a later rebase could
91	  invalidate.
92	
93	Use `[[N]]` refs — several as `[[N]],[[M]]` only when one
94	section records multiple commits (a merge non-ff close-out) —
95	with the commit URL + 40-hex SHA in the file's `# References`
96	(format in AGENTS.md
97	[Chores commit references](../AGENTS.md#chores-commit-references)).
98	A section's `##` title matches its commit title, so a rare
99	deliberate rewrite of a permanent-branch commit re-syncs via
100	`git log --grep "<title>"`.
101	
102	The per-push cadence is a project choice, not dogma — a
103	**per-close-out** model (recording a cycle's SHAs at its
104	close-out) is equally valid. The one invariant: a recorded
105	SHA must be permanent.
106	
107	## Preparation
108	
109	The cycle's first commit (`X.Y.Z-0`), when the cycle needs
110	setup (a lightweight cycle omits it — see
111	[versioning.md](versioning.md#step-numbering)):
112	
113	- **Bump the version-of-record** to `X.Y.Z-0`. Where it
114	  lives, the suffix scheme, and any derived files (a
115	  lockfile, a sourced manifest version) are
116	  project-specific — see [versioning.md](versioning.md).
117	- **Move a `## Todo` item** (if the cycle has one) into
118	  `## In Progress` and the todo item should have:
119	  - A **bold title line** — that will be the chores
120	    section header, minus the `## ` prefix.
121	  - A **succinct problem statement**; add if one is needed
122	  - A **plan ladder**.
123	- **Open the [chores section](#chores-sections)** —
124	  append a `##` header with the title it records — the
125	  cycle's anticipated close-out title.
126	
127	## Work-N
128	
129	The cycle's work commits (`X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, …)
130	implement the change. As many as needed:
131	
132	- Each commit runs through the
133	  **[per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow)**.
134	- **Interim pushes** are optional (backup, progress
135	  visibility).
136	- Close-out is the only mandatory push (see
137	  [Pushing](#pushing)).
138	- **Subdivide into a sub-cycle** if a Work commit's
139	  scope grows enough (see
140	  [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders)).
141	
142	## Close-out
143	
144	The cycle's last commit (bare `X.Y.Z`) does bookkeeping
145	only — the commit body describes that bookkeeping, not
146	what happens post-squash:
147	
148	- **Move the picked-up item** from `## In Progress` to a
149	  one-line entry (with a chores `[N]` ref) in `## Done`.
150	- **Move the `## In Progress` block into the
151	  [chores section](#chores-sections)** (the title-only
152	  placeholder opened during Preparation):
153	  - Cut the problem statement + plan ladder out of
154	    `## In Progress` (drop the bold title line — the
155	    chores `##` header already carries that string).
156	  - Paste under the chores `##` header.
157	  - Sync the chores header to the **final** commit title
158	    if the cycle's scope shifted; update every anchor
159	    back-reference.
160	  - Add an `### As-built ladder` listing the cycle's
161	    commits; optional `### Outcome` notes.
162	  - Replace `## In Progress` with
163	    `_No cycle currently in progress._`.
164	- **Update `notes/README.md`** if functionality changed
165	  (new flags, new subcommands, changed behavior).
166	
167	Whether to **squash** the cycle into one commit before the
168	publishing push, or push as-is, is decided at push time —
169	see [Pushing](#pushing).
170	
171	## Numbering
172	
173	Each commit's phase is encoded in the version suffix — `-0`
174	Preparation, `-1`/`-2`/… Work, bare `X.Y.Z` Close-out,
175	recursively for sub-cycles. The full scheme — disambiguation,
176	nesting, optional Preparation, the project's version-of-record
177	format, and the per-phase bump — lives in
178	[versioning.md](versioning.md#step-numbering), which is the
179	single source of truth for this repo's versioning.
180	
181	## Per-commit flow
182	
183	Every commit (Preparation, each Work commit, Close-out) goes
184	through:
185	
186	1. **Mark this commit `(current)`** as the first edit in
187	   `notes/todo.md > ## In Progress`.
188	2. **Do the work** (see [Iterative work](#iterative-work)
189	   for the loop-and-squash technique).
190	3. **Flip this commit `(current)` → `(done)`** in `## In
191	   Progress` — before the cargo cycle and the commit.
192	4. **Validate the artifact** — a medium-specific step, skip-able
193	   for notes-only commits, mandatory at close-out. For the Rust
194	   example the cargo cycle is:
195	   1. `cargo fmt`
196	   2. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`
197	   3. `cargo test`
198	   4. `cargo install --path . --locked`
199	   5. (re-test if anything substantive changed)
200	5. **Work review.** Stop *before* writing any description;
201	   tell the user "ready to commit." The user reviews the
202	   changes and we iterate until complete.
203	6. **Write the commit description** — see
204	   [Commit description](#commit-description).
205	7. **Commit Description review.** Show the title + body
206	   and stop. The user reviews the description. Iterate.
207	8. **Commit.** `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R .` for
208	   the app repo, `-R .claude` for the bot repo (`-R` last
209	   keeps the verb visible):
210	
211	   ```
212	   jj commit -m \
213	   "<type>: <short description>" \
214	   -m "<intro paragraph>
215	
216	   - file1: gist
217	   - file2: gist
218	
219	   ochid: /.claude/<chid>" \
220	   -R .
221	   ```
222	
223	**Two overrides apply:**
224	
225	- **Deviation or question** — any time the work deviates
226	  from the agreed plan, or a question arises, stop and
227	  surface it; don't push through.
228	- **ESC-ESC** — the user can interrupt at any point to pull
229	  a review or question forward.
230	
231	## Commit description
232	
233	[Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/):
234	
235	```
236	<type>: <short description>
237	```
238	
239	Titles carry **no trailing `(<version>)` suffix**. The
240	version-of-record — where it lives and its bump cadence, see
241	[versioning.md](versioning.md) — is useful for confirming
242	you're running the version you're testing, not a per-commit
243	title marker. We think per-commit version-in-title is
244	unstable on large projects: many changes are in flight
245	simultaneously, and a
246	version is only stable once merged into the main repo — so
247	titles don't carry one.
248	
249	### Title
250	
251	- ≤50 chars total.
252	- Common types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `test`,
253	  `docs`, `chore`.
254	- Favor terse phrasings.
255	- **Distinct per step** — each of a cycle's commits gets its
256	  own descriptive title (no shared cycle title with a step
257	  marker). Share a greppable stem across the cycle's titles
258	  (e.g. `ring buffer`) so `git log --grep` collects them; the
259	  chores section header matches the close-out title. See
260	  AGENTS.md
261	  [Conventional-commit shape](../AGENTS.md#conventional-commit-shape-ladder--chores--commit).
262	
263	### Body
264	
265	[Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form) (intro + bullets),
266	wrap ≤72. Bullet content differs per repo:
267	
268	- **App-repo body**: file-by-file. One bullet per file
269	  changed (file plus a one-line gist). Sub-bullets for
270	  files with multiple distinct changes:
271	
272	  ```
273	  - path/to/file1
274	    - first distinct change
275	    - second distinct change, wrapping to next line
276	       with continuation indented 5
277	  ```
278	
279	  The file-by-file list is the source of truth for the
280	  cycle's mechanical change record. Chores carries the
281	  narrative + design, not a copy of it. Promote any
282	  "why" beyond one sentence to a chores `###` subsection.
283	
284	- **Session-repo (`.claude`) body**: bullets describe
285	  in-session activity rather than code changes.
286	
287	### Trailer
288	
289	`ochid:` as the last line of the body — see
290	[Cross-repo linking (ochid trailers)](../AGENTS.md#cross-repo-linking-ochid-trailers)
291	in AGENTS.md for the convention.
292	
293	For breaking changes, use the hyphenated `BREAKING-CHANGE:`
294	trailer key. `BREAKING CHANGE:` (with a space) is the only
295	space-separated key the Conventional Commits spec allows; the
296	hyphenated form is also valid and avoids the space ambiguity.
297	
298	## Reviewing changes
299	
300	Work review looks at the **uncommitted working-copy diff**,
301	on the way to commit. The user opens diffs in their
302	editor (Zed, VSCode); jj commands are for terminal:
303	
304	- `jj diff` — working-copy diff (uncommitted)
305	- `jj diff -r @-` — diff of the previous commit
306	- `jj diff --from <X> --to <Y>` — any two revisions
307	- `jj show -r <X>` — description + diff for one rev
308	
309	Don't `jj edit -r @-` to view a past commit — that marks
310	it mutable and shifts `@`; use `jj diff -r @-` or
311	`jj show -r @-`.
312	
313	See [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders) for the
314	close-out squash recipe and recovery; revset primitives
315	are in [`jj-tips.md`](jj-tips.md#revsets).
316	
317	## Pushing
318	
319	### Policy
320	
321	Push is **discretionary** during the cycle (backup,
322	progress visibility) and **mandatory at close-out** —
323	the cycle's result must be published.
324	
325	**Approval is per-push.** Every push — any repo, any kind:
326	cycle push, interim backup, recovery/surgery force-push —
327	happens only after the user has reviewed the changes to be
328	published and explicitly approved that specific push.
329	Approval of a plan that *includes* a push does not authorize
330	the push itself; stop and ask again at the moment of pushing.
331	
332	**Default is interactive; an explicit scoped delegation waives
333	the gates.** The gates above — per-push approval, the
334	commit-description review (show title+body and stop), and the
335	hard stop after push/finalize — are the *interactive default*.
336	They yield when the user **explicitly** delegates a complete,
337	bounded task and authorizes carrying it through ("do all of X
338	and push each step, don't check in"). The bot then proceeds
339	through that task's commits and pushes without stopping, and
340	continues past each push to the next step. Conditions:
341	
342	- **Explicit grant** — never inferred from a task merely being
343	  well-scoped; the user's words must authorize unattended
344	  completion. "Commit and push" (or "then push") names the
345	  destination, not a waiver: it authorizes the push *after*
346	  the normal work review and description review, not skipping
347	  them. Only wording that explicitly waives the stops ("don't
348	  check in", "no need to review", "carry it through
349	  unattended") waives them.
350	- **Bounded goal** — covers the named task only; does not carry
351	  to the next task or a vaguer follow-on.
352	- **Destructive ops still pause** — delegation covers the task's
353	  ordinary commits and pushes; it does *not* pre-authorize a
354	  genuinely irreversible action (force-push over published
355	  history, history rewrite, deleting a remote branch). Those can
356	  permanently destroy work and aren't a normal cycle step, so the
357	  bot flags one before acting. An ordinary delegated cycle never
358	  reaches this.
359	- **Still transparent** — report each commit/push as it lands
360	  (title + outcome) so the user can catch up.
361	- **When in doubt, ask** — ambiguous authorization falls back to
362	  per-push approval.
363	
364	### Shape at close-out push
365	
366	At close-out the cycle's *work* is done; its *published
367	shape* is the remaining choice, made at push time. Surface
368	the options and get user approval before pushing — once on
369	the target, changing shape is a remote rewrite (force-push,
370	needs approval), so choose deliberately.
371	
372	- **Squash to one commit** — single entry on the target.
373	  Right for straightforward changes where the Work-N is
374	  focused on one or two files.
375	- **Merge non-ff** *(current default)* — `main` gains a
376	  merge commit (`X.Y.Z`); cycle commits stay reachable via
377	  two parents. `jj log -r ..@ -n <N>` shows the trapezoidal
378	  shape. See [Merge non-ff recipe](#merge-non-ff-recipe)
379	  for the full setup sequence.
380	- **Keep separate** — one commit per cycle entry on
381	  `main`. Use when the decomposition itself is
382	  informative. Each chores section keeps its own header /
383	  `Commits:` ref; no consolidation churn.
384	
385	Set up squash/merge before invoking `vc-x1 push`; use
386	`jj git push` directly for non-standard shapes.
387	
388	### Merge non-ff recipe
389	
390	Setting up a [Merge non-ff](#shape-at-close-out-push)
391	close-out is a fixed sequence. `<closeout>` is the cycle's
392	close-out commit, `<prev>` the previous cycle's close-out
393	(the current `main` tip), `<work-tip>` the cycle's last
394	Work commit:
395	
396	1. **Rebase the close-out into a merge** — `jj rebase -r
397	   <closeout> --onto <prev> --onto <work-tip>`.
398	   - `-r <closeout>` keeps the `<closeout>` commit in place.
399	   - `--onto <prev>` becomes its first parent (trunk).
400	   - `--onto <work-tip>` becomes the second parent.
401	
402	   Together these make `<closeout>` a merge of
403	   `<prev>` + `<work-tip>`, forming a trapezoidal commit.
404	2. **Use `jj new <merge>`** to add an empty `@` above the
405	   merge. The rebase left `@` *on* the now-content-bearing
406	   merge, which git/IDE diff views show as uncommitted;
407	   `jj new` restores the clean empty `@` on top.
408	3. **Push** — `jj git push --bookmark main -R .`.
409	
410	**Post-hoc caveat.** If the cycle was already pushed
411	[Keep separate](#shape-at-close-out-push) its commits are
412	immutable: the rebase needs `--ignore-immutable` and the
413	push force-updates `main`.
414	The standard sequence assumes the merge is set up *before*
415	the close-out push.
416	
417	### vc-x1 push wrapper
418	
419	`vc-x1 push <bookmark>` wraps per-push mechanics. See
420	`vc-x1 push --help` for current flags.
421	
422	**Current limitation**: only fully supports the
423	[Keep separate](#shape-at-close-out-push) shape; other
424	shapes need manual jj steps. Improvements tracked /
425	planned:
426	
427	- N:1 code↔bot for Merge non-ff (`## Todo` entry P1).
428	- Symmetric squash (planned, to be captured in `-2`).
429	- Per-repo bookmark names (planned).
430	
431	### .claude cadence
432	
433	**Cadence**: one push = one `.claude` commit, paired
434	with every code commit in that push.
435	
436	The `.claude` working copy accumulates session data
437	across the cycle; its change ID stays stable across
438	snapshots, `jj describe`, and the finalize commit, so
439	code-side `ochid:` trailers resolve.
440	
441	`.claude` is a linear journal — all session work lives
442	on `main`, regardless of the app-side bookmark. **Do not
443	create or maintain `.claude` bookmarks that mirror
444	app-side branches** — risks the bot steering session
445	pushes to the wrong remote ref.
446	
447	Ending a session: if the user runs `/exit` there will be
448	session information created, which we don't worry about.
449	The user can close the terminal instead and `@` will
450	remain empty.
451	
452	### Bot communication at the reviews
453	
454	Use plain prose — no insider jargon ("Gate N signal",
455	"Checkpoint N", etc.):
456	
457	- **At Work review** — summarize what changed and stop.
458	  "Work complete. Please review."
459	- **At Commit Description review** — present `$TITLE`
460	  and `$BODY` explicitly; ask permission to commit/push.
461	  Don't spell out the full `vc-x1 push ... --title ...
462	  --body ...` invocation by default.
463	- **At Post close-out review** — surface the shape
464	  options (squash / merge / keep) and the push target;
465	  wait for the user's choice before any `jj squash` /
466	  `jj rebase` / `jj git push` invocation.
467	
468	### After push or finalize: stop and wait
469	
470	After you **push** (cross the remote boundary) or launch
471	**finalize** on the `.claude` repo (`vc-x1 finalize --repo
472	.claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach`) — by
473	hand or via the `vc-x1 push` wrapper — you **MUST NEVER**
474	proceed (next step, edit, tool call, text output) until the
475	user explicitly directs you to continue. **Even when the next
476	step seems obvious — wait.** Treat push-or-finalize as a hard
477	stop for the whole turn.
478	
479	`vc-x1 push` performs both as its tail stages — `push-app`
480	then the detached `vc-x1 finalize` on `.claude` — so there is
481	no gap left to speak in once the push is invoked. Put **all**
482	closing words *before* invoking it. The harness rejects an empty
483	turn, so it may force a visible token after the tool returns; if
484	so, emit a bare acknowledgment only (e.g. "landed") — never a
485	summary, verification, or next-step offer.
486	
487	Why (`.claude` is the live journal finalize snapshots →
488	squashes → pushes):
489	
490	- **`--detach`**: lets the tool return immediately so the
491	  harness can keep flushing the *trailing* session-data — the
492	  tool call's own transcript and any words emitted *before*
493	  the invoke — into the snapshot.
494	- **`--delay 10`**: gives the harness time to complete that
495	  flush before the snapshot is taken. It is **not** time for
496	  the bot to do more work.
497	- **silence**: the bot must not summarize or perform any work
498	  after the tool returns. The harness may force a visible token
499	  (it rejects an empty turn) — keep it to a bare acknowledgment,
500	  never a summary or a next step.
501	
502	**Known slip**: the bot has emitted a summary after launching
503	finalize. There is no "harmless" closing line — if it's worth
504	saying, say it *before* the invoke.
505	
506	### Recovery
507	
508	- **If push exits before `finalize-claude`** (e.g.
509	  failure between `push-app` and `finalize-claude`), run
510	  finalize by hand:
511	
512	  ```
513	  vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
514	  ```
515	
516	- **Run finalize again if `@` is non-empty** after the
517	  finalize's squash-and-commit (also desirable after
518	  extra activity by the bot's agents).
519	  - Why: finalize snapshots `.claude` at `--delay`, but the
520	    bot keeps writing session data while it runs — the tool's
521	    own invocation plus the bot's closing response land
522	    *after* the snapshot.
523	  - Safe to repeat: bot session data is append-only, so a
524	    re-run never conflicts or overwrites. (This could
525	    change; it is not under the user's control.)
526	  - Fix: run `vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push
527	    <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach` another time to capture
528	    that tail; one extra pass is generally enough.
529	  - No guarantees: events outside the bot's control can leave
530	    `@` non-empty — e.g. the bot's back end may decide to
531	    squash/consolidate session data, which can take minutes
532	    and land after the snapshot. The remedy is the same: just
533	    run finalize again. This is why a single pass is never
534	    guaranteed to leave `@` empty.
535	- **Clear push's saved state** after any out-of-band
536	  recovery — `rm .vc-x1/push-state.toml` or `vc-x1 push
537	  <bookmark> --restart` — otherwise push resumes from a
538	  stale stage.
539	- **Late code-repo tweak after `push-app` succeeded**
540	  (e.g. updating AGENTS.md or memory) requires `jj
541	  squash --ignore-immutable` and a re-push; that is a
542	  remote rewrite and needs explicit approval like any
543	  push.
544	
545	## Iterative work
546	
547	When work for a single commit (the **target**) benefits
548	from incremental review, loop:
549	
550	1. `jj new -R .` — fresh empty `@` on top of the target.
551	2. Make the next round of changes.
552	3. User reviews the round (see
553	   [Reviewing changes](#reviewing-changes)).
554	4. `jj squash -R .` folds into the target and creates a
555	   new empty `@`.
556	5. If not done, go to step 2.
557	
558	Same jj mechanics as a
559	[sub-cycle ladder](#sub-cycle-ladders), but at
560	single-commit scope — the version
561	doesn't change.
562	
563	## Sub-cycle ladders
564	
565	When a Work commit subdivides into a sub-cycle (see
566	[Numbering](#numbering) for suffix nesting), its Work
567	commits typically live as a local jj `@` chain and
568	**collapse into the sub-cycle's Close-out** before the
569	parent cycle continues. Ladder commits are scratch —
570	for review and bisection only.
571	
572	### Per-Work-commit contract within a ladder
573	
574	For each Work commit in the ladder:
575	
576	1. `jj new -R .` — create a fresh empty `@`.
577	2. Do the commit's work.
578	3. Run the fast validation (Rust example: `cargo test
579	   --bins`). **Non-negotiable** — for code, build and clippy
580	   alone miss regressions until a later commit runs the full
581	   suite, raising bisection cost.
582	4. `jj describe -m "..." -m "..." -R .` — working title
583	   only; the sub-cycle Close-out collects everything
584	   into one final commit.
585	
586	### Navigating the ladder
587	
588	Common moves:
589	
590	- `jj log -r '<base>::' -R .` — see the whole ladder
591	  from its base.
592	- `jj edit -r <prefix> -R .` — jump `@` to any ladder
593	  commit by chid prefix; useful for bisection.
594	- `jj edit @-- -R .` — quick-jump back two commits.
595	- `jj diff -r <chid> -R .` — review one commit in
596	  isolation.
597	
598	Modifications to any ladder commit rewrite it in place;
599	descendants auto-rebase.
600	
601	### Close-out: squash the ladder
602	
603	When all ladder Work commits are done and tests pass:
604	
605	```
606	jj squash --from "<base>..@-" --into @ -u -R .
607	```
608	
609	`<base>` is the parent of the first ladder commit; `-u`
610	keeps `@`'s description and discards the sources'.
611	After squash, history is linear: `<base> → @`;
612	intermediate commits are auto-abandoned.
613	
614	Then `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` as for any other commit.
615	
616	For N = 1 the squash is a no-op (`<base>..@-` is empty
617	when `@-` is `<base>`); push the single commit directly.
618	
619	### Recovery
620	
621	If a ladder commit goes wrong, back out without losing
622	prior commits:
623	
624	- **Discard the current commit.** `jj abandon @ -R .`
625	  drops it; you get a fresh empty `@` on the same
626	  parent.
627	- **Edit an earlier commit.** `jj edit -r <chid> -R .`,
628	  make corrections, then `jj edit -r <last-ladder-chid>`
629	  to return. Descendants auto-rebase.
630	- **Discard the entire ladder.** `jj op log -R .` shows
631	  the op history; `jj op restore <op-id> -R .` reverts
632	  to that point. Full undo — removes *all* ladder work
633	  after the chosen op. Use only to start over.
634	
635	# References
636	
637	- [`jj-tips.md`](jj-tips.md#revsets) — revset primitives
638	  (chid/cid, `@`/`@-`/`@+`, `..`/`::` ranges, prefix matching).
639	- [`substep-test.sh`](substep-test.sh) — script that
640	  scaffolds a 4-revision ladder under `/tmp/substep-test`
641	  for squash-recipe experiments.
642	- The per-commit `cargo test --bins` gate exists because a
643	  regression introduced in an early ladder commit can go
644	  uncaught until a later commit runs the full suite, raising
645	  bisection cost.
646	
```

1	//! Generic bench driver: the [`Bench`] trait, adaptive outer/inner
2	//! loop sizing, and the band-histogram report.
3	
4	use std::hint::black_box;
5	
6	use hdrhistogram::Histogram;
7	
8	use crate::bands::{self, BandLabels};
9	use crate::overhead::Overhead;
10	
11	const WARMUP: u64 = 10_000;
12	const ESTIMATE_STEPS: u64 = 1_000;
13	const ESTIMATE_SAMPLES: usize = 5;
14	const FRAMING_DOMINATION_RATIO: f64 = 10.0;
15	const MAX_INNER: u64 = 1_000;
16	
17	/// Histogram value bounds: 1 ps to 60 s at 3 sig figs. Values
18	/// are recorded in **picoseconds** — the timer reads integer ns,
19	/// but dividing a sample by `inner` in ps keeps the true sub-ns
20	/// per-call precision that ns recording truncated (a 4.7 ns call
21	/// no longer rounds to 5). The high bound is a sane-world
22	/// ceiling for one recorded sample, not a technical limit —
23	/// [`record_sample`] clamps above it and [`warn_invalid`] flags
24	/// the run.
25	const HIST_LOW_PS: u64 = 1;
26	const HIST_HIGH_PS: u64 = 60_000_000_000_000;
27	
28	/// Picoseconds per nanosecond: recorded values are ps, display
29	/// is ns.
30	const PS_PER_NS: f64 = 1000.0;
31	
32	/// `CLOCK_BOOTTIME` minus `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` elapsed divergence
33	/// (seconds) at or above which [`warn_invalid`] reports that the
34	/// system suspended during the run.
35	const SUSPEND_WARN_S: f64 = 1.0;
36	
37	/// A benchmark workload: a named operation that `step()` performs
38	/// in a tight loop for sub-µs latency measurement. Implementors own
39	/// any setup state (channels, spawned threads, counters). `step()`
40	/// returns a value so the caller can `black_box` it against dead-code
41	/// elimination.
42	pub trait Bench {
43	    /// Human-readable name used in the report header.
44	    fn name(&self) -> &str;
45	
46	    /// Run one unit of work. Return any value derived from the work
47	    /// to defeat DCE — the caller black-boxes it.
48	    fn step(&mut self) -> u64;
49	}
50	
51	/// Runtime configuration for one [`run_adaptive`] call.
52	#[derive(Debug)]
53	pub struct RunCfg<'a> {
54	    /// Calibrated apparatus overhead (framing + loop/iter) used to
55	    /// compute the `adjusted` mean columns in the report.
56	    pub overhead: &'a Overhead,
57	    /// Wall-clock seconds budget for time-based runs. Ignored when
58	    /// `outer_override` is set.
59	    pub target_seconds: f64,
60	    /// Force a fixed outer-loop count, bypassing the time budget.
61	    pub outer_override: Option<u64>,
62	    /// Force a fixed inner-loop count, bypassing the
63	    /// overhead-dominated auto-sizing.
64	    pub inner_override: Option<u64>,
65	    /// Core pool for thread pinning. Indexed positionally with
66	    /// wrap-around via [`core_for`][RunCfg::core_for]; empty means
67	    /// no pinning.
68	    pub pin_cores: &'a [usize],
69	    /// When set, [`crate::tprobe::TProbe::report`] emits raw TSC
70	    /// ticks instead of nanoseconds. Plumbed from the `-t/--ticks`
71	    /// CLI flag.
72	    pub report_ticks: bool,
73	    /// Band-label style for [`print_report`] histogram rows.
74	    /// Plumbed from the `--band-labels` CLI flag.
75	    pub band_labels: BandLabels,
76	    /// Decimal digits on [`print_report`] time columns. Plumbed
77	    /// from the `--decimals` CLI flag (default 1; 0 restores
78	    /// integers; 3 is the ps recording floor).
79	    pub decimals: usize,
80	}
81	
82	impl RunCfg<'_> {
83	    /// CPU id for the bench's `thread_idx`-th thread, using
84	    /// wrap-around over the pool. Returns `None` when the pool is
85	    /// empty so callers can treat unpinned and pinned runs uniformly.
86	    pub fn core_for(&self, thread_idx: usize) -> Option<usize> {
87	        if self.pin_cores.is_empty() {
88	            None
89	        } else {
90	            Some(self.pin_cores[thread_idx % self.pin_cores.len()])
91	        }
92	    }
93	}
94	
95	/// Drive `bench` against `cfg` and return
96	/// `(histogram, outer, inner, duration_s, suspended_s)`.
97	///
98	/// After a fixed warmup, `inner` is auto-sized so apparatus framing
99	/// doesn't dominate (skipped when `cfg.inner_override` is set). The
100	/// outer loop runs either for `cfg.outer_override` iterations or
101	/// until `cfg.target_seconds` elapses. `suspended_s` is the time
102	/// the system spent suspended during the measured run (see
103	/// [`ClockPair`]); pass it to [`print_report`], which flags the
104	/// poisoned stats when it is non-trivial.
105	pub fn run_adaptive<B: Bench>(bench: &mut B, cfg: &RunCfg) -> (Histogram<u64>, u64, u64, f64, f64) {
106	    for _ in 0..WARMUP {
107	        black_box(bench.step());
108	    }
109	
110	    let step_cost_ns = estimate_step_cost(bench);
111	    let framing_ns = cfg.overhead.framing_per_sample_ns.max(1.0);
112	    let inner = cfg
113	        .inner_override
114	        .unwrap_or_else(|| pick_inner(step_cost_ns, framing_ns));
115	
116	    let clocks = ClockPair::now();
117	    let (hist, outer, duration_s) = match cfg.outer_override {
118	        Some(outer) => {
119	            let (hist, duration_s) = run_counted(bench, outer, inner);

1	//! Thread CPU-pinning helpers: `--pin` parsing, affinity snapshot
2	//! and restore, and human-readable mask/plan summaries.
3	
4	use std::collections::BTreeSet;
5	
6	/// Parse a `--pin` value (comma-separated list with optional ranges) into
7	/// an ordered vector of logical CPU IDs. Accepts forms like `"0,1"`,
8	/// `"0-11"`, `"0,3-5,7"`. Duplicates are preserved in the given order so
9	/// oversubscription (`"0,0,0"`) works.
10	pub fn parse_cores(spec: &str) -> Result<Vec<usize>, String> {
11	    let mut out = Vec::new();
12	    for part in spec.split(',').map(str::trim).filter(|s| !s.is_empty()) {
13	        match part.split_once('-') {
14	            None => out.push(
15	                part.parse::<usize>()
16	                    .map_err(|e| format!("invalid core id {part:?}: {e}"))?,
17	            ),
18	            Some((lo, hi)) => {
19	                let lo = lo
20	                    .trim()
21	                    .parse::<usize>()
22	                    .map_err(|e| format!("invalid range start {lo:?}: {e}"))?;
23	                let hi = hi
24	                    .trim()
25	                    .parse::<usize>()
26	                    .map_err(|e| format!("invalid range end {hi:?}: {e}"))?;
27	                if hi < lo {
28	                    return Err(format!("range {lo}-{hi} is empty"));
29	                }
30	                out.extend(lo..=hi);
31	            }
32	        }
33	    }
34	    Ok(out)
35	}
36	
37	/// Print the current thread's CPU id to stderr with a label.
38	/// Useful for debugging pinning — not called in normal paths.
39	#[allow(dead_code)]
40	pub fn print_core_id(prompt: &str) {
41	    let cid = unsafe { libc::sched_getcpu() };
42	    eprintln!("{prompt}: cid={cid}");
43	}
44	
45	/// Pin the current thread to `logical_cpu`. No-op if `None`.
46	///
47	/// Constructs a `CoreId` directly rather than querying
48	/// `get_core_ids()`, which only returns cores in the caller's
49	/// current affinity mask — after the first pin that mask is
50	/// narrowed and subsequent lookups for other cores would fail.
51	pub fn pin_current(logical_cpu: Option<usize>) {
52	    let Some(target) = logical_cpu else { return };
53	    core_affinity::set_for_current(core_affinity::CoreId { id: target });
54	}
55	
56	/// Read the current thread's CPU affinity mask, or `None` if the
57	/// syscall fails (very unusual on Linux).
58	pub fn current_affinity() -> Option<libc::cpu_set_t> {
59	    let mut set: libc::cpu_set_t = unsafe { std::mem::zeroed() };
60	    let ret = unsafe { libc::sched_getaffinity(0, size_of::<libc::cpu_set_t>(), &mut set) };
61	    (ret == 0).then_some(set)
62	}
63	
64	/// Snapshot the current thread's affinity mask so it can be restored
65	/// later. Logs the captured mask at `info` level. Returns `None` on
66	/// syscall failure (very unusual on Linux).
67	pub fn save_affinity() -> Option<libc::cpu_set_t> {
68	    match current_affinity() {
69	        Some(set) => {
70	            log::info!("save_affinity: mask={}", affinity_summary(&set));
71	            Some(set)
72	        }
73	        None => {
74	            log::warn!("save_affinity: sched_getaffinity failed");
75	            None
76	        }
77	    }
78	}
79	
80	/// Restore a previously-saved affinity mask. Used after calibration
81	/// to widen the mask back to what we started with (typically "all
82	/// CPUs the process was launched with", preserving any outer
83	/// `taskset` restrictions). Logs the restored mask at `info` level.
84	pub fn restore_affinity(set: &libc::cpu_set_t) {
85	    let ret = unsafe { libc::sched_setaffinity(0, size_of::<libc::cpu_set_t>(), set) };
86	    if ret == 0 {
87	        log::info!("restore_affinity: mask={}", affinity_summary(set));
88	    } else {
89	        log::warn!("restore_affinity: sched_setaffinity failed");
90	    }
91	}
92	
93	/// Format a CPU set as a compact range list with a count suffix,
94	/// e.g. `"0-11,13-15 (15 cpus)"` or `"5 (1 cpu)"`.
95	pub fn affinity_summary(set: &libc::cpu_set_t) -> String {
96	    let cpus: Vec<usize> = (0..libc::CPU_SETSIZE as usize)
97	        .filter(|&i| unsafe { libc::CPU_ISSET(i, set) })
98	        .collect();
99	    if cpus.is_empty() {
100	        return "<empty>".to_string();
101	    }
102	    let mut ranges: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
103	    let mut start = cpus[0];
104	    let mut prev = cpus[0];
105	    for &c in &cpus[1..] {
106	        if c == prev + 1 {
107	            prev = c;
108	        } else {
109	            ranges.push(if start == prev {
110	                start.to_string()
111	            } else {
112	                format!("{start}-{prev}")
113	            });
114	            start = c;
115	            prev = c;
116	        }
117	    }
118	    ranges.push(if start == prev {
119	        start.to_string()
120	    } else {
121	        format!("{start}-{prev}")
122	    });
123	    format!(
124	        "{} ({} cpu{})",
125	        ranges.join(","),
126	        cpus.len(),
127	        if cpus.len() == 1 { "" } else { "s" }
128	    )
129	}
130	
131	/// Report the pinning plan (what the user asked for) as a human-readable
132	/// summary for the startup banner.
133	pub fn plan_summary(cores: &[usize]) -> String {
134	    if cores.is_empty() {
135	        return "none (unpinned)".to_string();
136	    }
137	    let unique: BTreeSet<usize> = cores.iter().copied().collect();
138	    format!(
139	        "{cores:?} ({} slot{}, {} unique CPU{})",
140	        cores.len(),
141	        if cores.len() == 1 { "" } else { "s" },
142	        unique.len(),
143	        if unique.len() == 1 { "" } else { "s" },
144	    )
145	}
146	
147	#[cfg(test)]
148	mod tests {
149	    use super::*;
150	
151	    #[test]
152	    fn parse_plain_list() {
153	        assert_eq!(parse_cores("0,1,2").unwrap(), vec![0, 1, 2]);
154	    }
155	
156	    #[test]
157	    fn parse_range() {
158	        assert_eq!(parse_cores("0-3").unwrap(), vec![0, 1, 2, 3]);
159	    }
160	
161	    #[test]
162	    fn parse_mixed() {
163	        assert_eq!(parse_cores("0,3-5,7").unwrap(), vec![0, 3, 4, 5, 7]);
164	    }
165	
166	    #[test]
167	    fn parse_duplicates_preserved() {
168	        assert_eq!(parse_cores("0,0,0").unwrap(), vec![0, 0, 0]);
169	    }
170	
171	    #[test]
172	    fn parse_empty_string_ok() {
173	        assert_eq!(parse_cores("").unwrap(), Vec::<usize>::new());
174	    }
175	
176	    #[test]
177	    fn parse_reverse_range_errs() {
178	        assert!(parse_cores("5-3").is_err());
179	    }
180	
181	    #[test]
182	    fn parse_garbage_errs() {
183	        assert!(parse_cores("abc").is_err());
184	        assert!(parse_cores("1-x").is_err());
185	    }
186	
187	    #[test]
188	    fn pin_current_can_switch_cores() {
189	        let mut set = unsafe { std::mem::zeroed::<libc::cpu_set_t>() };
190	        let ret = unsafe { libc::sched_getaffinity(0, size_of::<libc::cpu_set_t>(), &mut set) };
191	        if ret != 0 {
192	            eprintln!("can't query affinity");
193	            return; // can't query affinity
194	        }
195	        let available = unsafe { libc::CPU_COUNT(&set) } as usize;
196	        if available < 2 {
197	            eprintln!("single-core or restricted by taskset");
198	            return; // single-core or restricted by taskset
199	        }
200	        let a = 0;
201	        let b = 1;
202	
203	        pin_current(Some(a));
204	        super::print_core_id("after pin to a");
205	        let cpu_a = unsafe { libc::sched_getcpu() } as usize;
206	        assert_eq!(cpu_a, a);
207	
208	        pin_current(Some(b));
209	        super::print_core_id("after pin to b");
210	        let cpu_b = unsafe { libc::sched_getcpu() } as usize;
211	        assert_eq!(cpu_b, b);
212	    }
213	}
214

> AGENT

I've got the full picture now. Before opening the cycle, here's the design and ladder I'm proposing, plus a couple of forks I want your call on.

## The feature

A layered config with strict precedence: **built-in defaults < `~/.config/iiac-perf/config.toml` (XDG) < project-local file < CLI flags**. TOML schema:

```toml
duration     = 5.0
band_labels  = "both"
decimals     = 1

[profiles]           # named pin lists
smt = "0,12"         # SMT siblings, max contention
ccx = "0,1"          # same CCX, independent cores
ccd = "0,6"          # cross-CCD
```

Then `--pin smt` resolves `smt` → `"0,12"` → cores via the existing `parse_cores`. Raw `--pin 0,12` still works (profile lookup first, fall back to parse).

Mechanics: add `serde` + `toml` deps, a new `src/config.rs` (load + merge, no `dirs` crate — resolve `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME`/`$HOME` by hand). To make "CLI always wins" work cleanly, `--band-labels`/`--decimals` lose their `default_value_t` and become `Option`, resolved against the merged config so I can tell "user passed it" from "defaulted."

## Proposed ladder (0.15.0)

- `-0` Prep: bump version, move todo → In Progress, open chores section
- `-1` `src/config.rs` + serde/toml deps — load/merge/precedence, unit tests, no wiring
- `-2` wire config into `main` — duration/band_labels/decimals defaults from config
- `-3` named pin profiles — `--pin <name>` resolves via `[profiles]`
- `0.15.0` close-out — README config section + bookkeeping

Two design forks I want your call on before I open it:

1	//! Report band boundaries and label styles: the single source of
2	//! truth for the min/z/p/n/max ladder that
3	//! [`crate::harness::print_report`] renders, documented by the
4	//! README's boundary-ladder table (pinned by this module's tests).
5	//!
6	//! - Familiar deciles in the body; nines/zeros tails generated
7	//!   from [`Z_DEPTH`]/[`N_DEPTH`]. Fractions and names come from
8	//!   one structural description, so the label styles can never
9	//!   drift apart and deepening a tail is a one-constant change.
10	//! - `band_table.rs` and `probe.rs` still carry their own older
11	//!   percentile ladders; unifying them onto this module is part
12	//!   of the probe-based-harness conversion todo.
13	
14	/// Fast-tail depth: the lowest tail boundary is `z{Z_DEPTH}`.
15	const Z_DEPTH: i32 = 4;
16	/// Slow-tail depth: the highest tail boundary is `n{N_DEPTH}`.
17	/// Deeper than [`Z_DEPTH`] because a latency distribution is
18	/// floored below (nothing beats the fast path) and open above.
19	const N_DEPTH: i32 = 10;
20	
21	/// Band-label style for the report's histogram rows, selected by
22	/// the `--band-labels` CLI flag.
23	///
24	/// - `Zpn` — nines/zeros + decile names (`z3`, `p50`, `n4`).
25	/// - `Frac` — literal boundary fractions with `_` grouping
26	///   (`0.001`, `0.50`, `0.999_9`).
27	/// - `Both` — zpn and fraction side by side (`n4  0.999_9`);
28	///   the default: the juxtaposition teaches the zpn vocabulary.
29	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, clap::ValueEnum)]
30	pub enum BandLabels {
31	    Zpn,
32	    Frac,
33	    Both,
34	}
35	
36	impl BandLabels {
37	    /// Lowercase name used on the CLI and in the report header's
38	    /// `labels=` metadata.
39	    pub fn as_str(self) -> &'static str {
40	        match self {
41	            BandLabels::Zpn => "zpn",
42	            BandLabels::Frac => "frac",
43	            BandLabels::Both => "both",
44	        }
45	    }
46	
47	    /// Trimmed-stat row range label matching the style, so `n2`
48	    /// never appears in a frac-only table.
49	    pub fn trim_label(self) -> &'static str {
50	        match self {
51	            BandLabels::Frac => "min..0.99",
52	            BandLabels::Zpn | BandLabels::Both => "min..n2",
53	        }
54	    }
55	}
56	
57	/// One report band boundary: its CDF fraction plus its rendered
58	/// name in each label style.
59	pub struct Boundary {
60	    /// Fraction of samples at or below the boundary (CDF).
61	    pub pct: f64,
62	    /// nines/zeros / decile name (`z3`, `p50`, `n4`).
63	    pub zpn: String,
64	    /// Literal-fraction name, `_`-grouped (`0.001`, `0.999_9`).
65	    pub frac: String,
66	}
67	
68	impl Boundary {
69	    /// Row label for this boundary in the given style. min/max
70	    /// carry no fraction, so `both` prints them bare; elsewhere
71	    /// `both` pads zpn to its 3-char max so the fraction column
72	    /// aligns.
73	    pub fn label(&self, style: BandLabels) -> String {
74	        match style {
75	            BandLabels::Zpn => self.zpn.clone(),
76	            BandLabels::Frac => self.frac.clone(),
77	            BandLabels::Both if self.zpn == self.frac => self.zpn.clone(),
78	            BandLabels::Both => format!("{:<3} {}", self.zpn, self.frac),
79	        }
80	    }

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/config.rs",
  "content": "//! Layered configuration for defaults: built-in < XDG config file <\n//! project-local file < CLI flags.\n//!\n//! - **XDG file** — `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/iiac-perf/config.toml`, or\n//!   `$HOME/.config/iiac-perf/config.toml` when `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`\n//!   is unset. The per-user home for defaults and pin profiles.\n//! - **Project-local file** — [`LOCAL_FILE`] in the current\n//!   directory (no upward walk). Overrides the XDG file\n//!   field-by-field; profiles merge by key.\n//! - **CLI** — always wins, resolved in `main` after [`load`].\n//!\n//! A malformed or unreadable-but-present file is a hard error, so a\n//! typo surfaces rather than silently reverting to built-ins.\n\nuse std::collections::BTreeMap;\nuse std::path::PathBuf;\n\nuse serde::Deserialize;\n\nuse crate::bands::BandLabels;\n\n/// Project-local override filename, looked up in the current\n/// directory only.\nconst LOCAL_FILE: &str = \"iiac-perf.toml\";\n\n/// Inclusive upper bound on `decimals`, mirroring the\n/// `--decimals` CLI `value_parser` range.\nconst DECIMALS_MAX: u8 = 3;\n\n/// The on-disk TOML shape, before validation. Scalars are\n/// `Option` so an absent key stays absent (letting a lower layer\n/// or built-in default show through); unknown keys are rejected\n/// to catch typos.\n#[derive(Debug, Default, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]\nstruct RawConfig {\n    /// Default `--duration` seconds.\n    duration: Option<f64>,\n    /// Default `--band-labels` style, as its lowercase name.\n    band_labels: Option<String>,\n    /// Default `--decimals` count.\n    decimals: Option<u8>,\n    /// Named pin profiles: name -> `--pin` core spec.\n    #[serde(default)]\n    profiles: BTreeMap<String, String>,\n}\n\n/// The merged, validated configuration handed to `main`.\n///\n/// Each scalar is `Option`: `None` means \"no config opinion, use\n/// the built-in default\". Profiles are a flat name->spec map.\n#[derive(Debug, Default, PartialEq)]\npub struct Config {\n    /// Default `--duration` seconds, if configured.\n    pub duration: Option<f64>,\n    /// Default `--band-labels` style, if configured.\n    pub band_labels: Option<BandLabels>,\n    /// Default `--decimals` count, if configured.\n    pub decimals: Option<u8>,\n    /// Named pin profiles: name -> `--pin` core spec.\n    pub profiles: BTreeMap<String, String>,\n}\n\nimpl Config {\n    /// Resolve a `--pin` spec against the configured profiles: a\n    /// spec that names a profile expands to that profile's core\n    /// spec; anything else is returned unchanged for\n    /// [`crate::pin::parse_cores`] to parse as a raw core list.\n    pub fn resolve_pin<'a>(&'a self, spec: &'a str) -> &'a str {\n        self.profiles.get(spec).map(String::as_str).unwrap_or(spec)\n    }\n}\n\n/// The XDG config-file path: `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/iiac-perf/config.toml`,\n/// falling back to `$HOME/.config/...`. `None` if neither env var is\n/// set (e.g. a stripped-down service environment).\nfn xdg_path() -> Option<PathBuf> {\n    let base = match std::env::var_os(\"XDG_CONFIG_HOME\") {\n        Some(x) if !x.is_empty() => PathBuf::from(x),\n        _ => PathBuf::from(std::env::var_os(\"HOME\")?).join(\".config\"),\n    };\n    Some(base.join(\"iiac-perf\").join(\"config.toml\"))\n}\n\n/// Load and merge the XDG and project-local config files. Returns\n/// the built-in-default [`Config`] when no file exists; errors on a\n/// present-but-unreadable or malformed file.\n///\n/// Layering: start from the XDG file, then overlay the local file\n/// (scalars replace, profiles merge by key), so the nearer file\n/// wins per field.\npub fn load() -> Result<Config, String> {\n    let mut raw = RawConfig::default();\n    if let Some(path) = xdg_path() {\n        overlay(&mut raw, &path)?;\n    }\n    overlay(&mut raw, &PathBuf::from(LOCAL_FILE))?;\n    validate(raw)\n}\n\n/// Read one config file (if it exists) and overlay it onto `base`:\n/// each present scalar replaces `base`'s, and profiles are merged\n/// by key (the file's entries win). A missing file is a no-op.\nfn overlay(base: &mut RawConfig, path: &PathBuf) -> Result<(), String> {\n    let text = match std::fs::read_to_string(path) {\n        Ok(t) => t,\n        Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => return Ok(()),\n        Err(e) => return Err(format!(\"reading {}: {e}\", path.display())),\n    };\n    let over: RawConfig =\n        toml::from_str(&text).map_err(|e| format!(\"parsing {}: {e}\", path.display()))?;\n    if over.duration.is_some() {\n        base.duration = over.duration;\n    }\n    if over.band_labels.is_some() {\n        base.band_labels = over.band_labels;\n    }\n    if over.decimals.is_some() {\n        base.decimals = over.decimals;\n    }\n    base.profiles.extend(over.profiles);\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Validate a merged [`RawConfig`] into a [`Config`]: map the\n/// `band_labels` name to the enum and range-check `decimals`.\nfn validate(raw: RawConfig) -> Result<Config, String> {\n    let band_labels = match raw.band_labels {\n        None => None,\n        Some(s) => Some(match s.as_str() {\n            \"zpn\" => BandLabels::Zpn,\n            \"frac\" => BandLabels::Frac,\n            \"both\" => BandLabels::Both,\n            other => {\n                return Err(format!(\n                    \"band_labels: {other:?} is not one of zpn, frac, both\"\n                ));\n            }\n        }),\n    };\n    if let Some(d) = raw.decimals\n        && d > DECIMALS_MAX\n    {\n        return Err(format!(\"decimals: {d} exceeds the maximum of {DECIMALS_MAX}\"));\n    }\n    Ok(Config {\n        duration: raw.duration,\n        band_labels,\n        decimals: raw.decimals,\n        profiles: raw.profiles,\n    })\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    fn parse(text: &str) -> Result<Config, String> {\n        validate(toml::from_str(text).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?)\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn empty_is_all_none() {\n        assert_eq!(parse(\"\").unwrap(), Config::default());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn scalars_parse() {\n        let c = parse(\"duration = 2.5\\nband_labels = \\\"zpn\\\"\\ndecimals = 0\\n\").unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(c.duration, Some(2.5));\n        assert_eq!(c.band_labels, Some(BandLabels::Zpn));\n        assert_eq!(c.decimals, Some(0));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn profiles_parse_and_resolve() {\n        let c = parse(\"[profiles]\\nsmt = \\\"0,12\\\"\\nccx = \\\"0,1\\\"\\n\").unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(c.resolve_pin(\"smt\"), \"0,12\");\n        assert_eq!(c.resolve_pin(\"ccx\"), \"0,1\");\n        // A non-profile spec passes through untouched.\n        assert_eq!(c.resolve_pin(\"0,3-5\"), \"0,3-5\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn bad_band_labels_errs() {\n        assert!(parse(\"band_labels = \\\"nope\\\"\\n\").is_err());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn out_of_range_decimals_errs() {\n        assert!(parse(\"decimals = 5\\n\").is_err());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn unknown_key_errs() {\n        assert!(parse(\"bogus = 1\\n\").is_err());\n    }\n}\n"
}

}

On 3900X,\n    /// logical CPUs N and N+12 are SMT siblings of the same physical\n    /// core — `--pin 0,12` pairs siblings (max contention), `--pin 0,1`\n    /// gives independent cores. Omit to leave threads unpinned.\n    #[arg(long, value_name = \"CORES\")]\n    pin: Option<String>,",

On 3900X,\n    /// logical CPUs N and N+12 are SMT siblings of the same physical\n    /// core — `--pin 0,12` pairs siblings (max contention), `--pin 0,1`\n    /// gives independent cores. A value naming a `[profiles]` entry in\n    /// the config file expands to that profile's core spec (e.g.\n    /// `--pin smt`). Omit to leave threads unpinned.\n    #[arg(long, value_name = \"CORES\")]\n    pin: Option<String>,"

  "old_string": "    let target_seconds = match (cli.duration, cli.total_duration) {\n        (Some(d), _) => d,\n        (None, Some(t)) => t / runners.len() as f64,\n        (None, None) => DEFAULT_DURATION,\n    };\n\n    let cfg = harness::RunCfg {\n        overhead: &overhead,\n        target_seconds,\n        outer_override: cli.outer,\n        inner_override: cli.inner,\n        pin_cores: &pin_cores,\n        report_ticks: cli.ticks,\n        band_labels: cli.band_labels,\n        decimals: cli.decimals as usize,\n    };",
  "new_string": "    // Duration precedence: CLI -d / -D win, then the config\n    // `duration`, then the built-in default.\n    let target_seconds = match (cli.duration, cli.total_duration) {\n        (Some(d), _) => d,\n        (None, Some(t)) => t / runners.len() as f64,\n        (None, None) => config.duration.unwrap_or(DEFAULT_DURATION),\n    };\n\n    let cfg = harness::RunCfg {\n        overhead: &overhead,\n        target_seconds,\n        outer_override: cli.outer,\n        inner_override: cli.inner,\n        pin_cores: &pin_cores,\n        report_ticks: cli.ticks,\n        band_labels: cli\n            .band_labels\n            .or(config.band_labels)\n            .unwrap_or(DEFAULT_BAND_LABELS),\n        decimals: cli.decimals.or(config.decimals).unwrap_or(DEFAULT_DECIMALS) as usize,\n    };"
}

51	## Usage
52	
53	```
54	iiac-perf [BENCH...] [-d SECONDS] [-o OUTER] [-i INNER]
55	```
56	
57	`BENCH` is one or more registered bench names, or `all` for every
58	registered bench. A name that matches no bench exactly runs every
59	bench it is a prefix of — `ice` runs all iceoryx2 benches, `mpsc`
60	runs `mpsc-1t` and `mpsc-2t`. **With no arguments, `iiac-perf` prints the
61	available list and exits — that's the source of truth for which
62	benches the current build registers.**
63	
64	Flags (also visible via `-h` / `--help`):
65	- `-d`, `--duration SECONDS` — target wall-clock seconds per bench
66	  (default `5.0`); the outer loop runs until this time is reached
67	  (inner auto-sizes). See chores `0.3.1-dev1` for the empirical
68	  study behind the default — longer (`-d 30`+) gives
69	  publication-grade stability. Mutually exclusive with `-D`.
70	- `-D`, `--total-duration SECONDS` — target total wall-clock seconds
71	  across all requested benches; budget is split equally per bench
72	  (e.g. `-D 30` with 6 benches → 5 s each). Mutually exclusive with
73	  `-d`.
74	- `-o`, `--outer N` — override outer loop count (forces count-based
75	  mode instead of time-based; inner still adapts).
76	- `-i`, `--inner N` — override inner loop count per histogram sample.
77	  `inner=1` measures single-call latency (each sample = one step).
78	  Higher inner measures back-to-back / burst rate (each sample = N
79	  steps averaged, hides per-call jitter and parking costs).
80	- `--pin CORES` — pin bench threads to logical CPUs. `CORES` is a
81	  comma-separated list with optional ranges: `0,1`, `0-5`, `0,3-5,7`.
82	  Treated as a **core pool** indexed positionally with wrap-around, so
83	  thread `i` gets `pool[i % pool.len()]`. Examples:
84	  - `--pin 0,1` pins a 2-thread bench to logical CPUs 0 and 1.
85	  - `--pin 0,0` co-locates two threads on the same CPU
86	    (oversubscription — measures contention).
87	  - `--pin 0-11` defines a 12-CPU pool for larger fanout benches;
88	    threads wrap over it.
89	
90	  On AMD Zen 2 (e.g. Ryzen 9 3900X, 12 physical × 2 SMT = 24 logical
91	  CPUs), logical IDs `N` and `N+12` are SMT siblings of the same
92	  physical core. `--pin 0,12` pairs siblings (max resource contention);
93	  `--pin 0,1` uses independent physical cores in the same CCX (best
94	  latency for channel benches — shared L3, no SMT contention). Check
95	  your topology with
96	  `cat /sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu0/topology/thread_siblings_list`.
97	
98	  Typical measured effect on `mpsc-2t` at `-d 10` (3900X, idle desktop):
99	  unpinned mean ≈ 7,044 ns / stdev ≈ 6,545 ns / p99.99 ≈ 74 µs;
100	  `--pin 0,1` → mean ≈ 5,636 ns / stdev ≈ 1,321 ns / p99.99 ≈ 17 µs.
101	  Tail tightens ~4×, stdev ~5×, mean drops ~20 %.
102	- `--no-pin-cal` — skip pinning the main thread for calibration.
103	  By default, calibration runs with main pinned to `pin[0]` (if
104	  `--pin` is set) or core 0, so framing/loop numbers come from a
105	  stable environment regardless of `--pin`. Pass this flag to
106	  reproduce pre-0.6.0 behavior (main pinned iff `--pin` is given)
107	  for A/B comparison. No effect when `--pin` is set.
108	- `-v`, `--verbose` — print internals to stderr (affinity mask at
109	  startup, calibration parameters, raw `min_low`/`min_high`,
110	  precise fit values, calibration wall time). Equivalent to
111	  `RUST_LOG=debug`. Default filter is `warn` — silent unless
112	  something is wrong. `RUST_LOG`, when set, wins over `-v` so
113	  per-module filtering still works.
114	- `--band-labels STYLE` — label style for the histogram rows:
115	  `zpn` (nines/zeros + decile names: `z3`, `p50`, `n4`), `frac`
116	  (literal boundary fractions with `_` grouping: `0.001`, `0.50`,
117	  `0.999_9`), or `both` (default) — zpn and fraction side by
118	  side; the juxtaposition teaches the zpn vocabulary, switch to
119	  `zpn` once fluent. The report header records the active style
120	  as `labels=<style>` so saved outputs are self-describing.
121	- `--decimals N` — decimal digits on the report's time columns
122	  (0–3). Default 1 shows the sub-ns precision that picosecond
123	  recording captures (values are recorded internally in ps and
124	  displayed in ns); `0` restores integer ns; `3` is the
125	  recording floor — more digits would be artifacts.
126	- `--no-inhibit` — do not inhibit system sleep for the run. By
127	  default the process re-execs itself under
128	  `systemd-inhibit --what=sleep` so an idle-suspend can't poison a
129	  long measurement (a mid-sample suspend inflates that sample by
130	  the whole sleep gap; see the `WARNING` lines below). Where
131	  `systemd-inhibit` is unavailable the run continues uninhibited
132	  and the banner's `sleep inhibit` line says so. Pass this flag to
133	  keep the process image untouched (strace/gdb/perf wrappers), to
134	  let the machine sleep on purpose, or to test the
135	  suspend-detection path — a sleep inhibitor also blocks manual
136	  `systemctl suspend`.
137	- `-t`, `--ticks` — show `TProbe` results in raw hardware tick
138	  counts (`tk`; x86_64 TSC, aarch64 `CNTVCT_EL0`) instead of
139	  converting to nanoseconds. Only affects `TProbe`-based benches
140	  (e.g. `tp-pc`); `Probe`-based output is always in nanoseconds.
141	  Use this to inspect the underlying tick counts directly, e.g.
142	  when comparing against the counter frequency.
143	
144	Each bench prints a band-based histogram in nanoseconds. Each row
145	is one band, labeled by its **upper boundary**, the lower boundary
146	being the previous printed row: deciles in the body (`p10` …
147	`p90`) and **nines/zeros** notation in both tails, where `nK`/`zK`
148	mark the boundary with a fraction 10<sup>-K</sup> of samples above
149	(`n`) or below (`z`) it — so `n2` ≡ p99, `n3` ≡ p99.9, … `n10`,
150	and `z2` ≡ p1, `z3` ≡ p0.1, `z4`. "K nines" is standard
151	engineering shorthand for proportions near one
152	([Nines (notation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nines_%28notation%29),
153	nines = −log₁₀(1−x)); `zK` is this project's mirror of it for the
154	fast tail (the underlying concept is the
155	[survival function](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Survival_function)
156	/ CCDF tail fraction). The slow tail subdivides down to `n10`, the
157	fast tail only to `z4` — a latency distribution is floored below
158	(nothing beats the fast path) and open above. A band only prints
159	when it has samples, so deep tail rows appear as run length earns
160	them (populating `n10` takes ~1e10 calls). Each row shows first,
161	last, range (`last - first + 1`), count, mean, and adjusted mean.
162	The trimmed `min..n2` rows exclude every band at or above
163	`n2` (p99).
164	
165	The full boundary ladder across its range (label styles per
166	`--band-labels`). The ladder is generated by
167	[`src/bands.rs`](src/bands.rs) — the single source of truth for
168	boundaries and labels — and this table is pinned by that module's
169	unit test, so code and docs can't silently drift:
170	
171	| zpn       | frac              | ≡ percentile    | tail fraction |
172	|-----------|-------------------|-----------------|---------------|
173	| `z4`      | `0.000_1`         | p0.01           | 1e-4 below    |
174	| `z3`      | `0.001`           | p0.1            | 1e-3 below    |
175	| `z2`      | `0.01`            | p1              | 1e-2 below    |
176	| `p10`–`p90` | `0.10`–`0.90`   | deciles         | —             |
177	| `n2`      | `0.99`            | p99             | 1e-2 above    |
178	| `n3`      | `0.999`           | p99.9           | 1e-3 above    |
179	| `n4`      | `0.999_9`         | p99.99          | 1e-4 above    |
180	| `n5`      | `0.999_99`        | p99.999         | 1e-5 above    |
```

  "new_string": "### Config file\n\nDefaults and named pin profiles can live in a TOML config file, so\ncommon invocations don't repeat flags. Precedence, lowest to\nhighest:\n\n- **built-in defaults** — `duration=5.0`, `band_labels=both`,\n  `decimals=1`;\n- **XDG file** — `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/iiac-perf/config.toml`, or\n  `$HOME/.config/iiac-perf/config.toml` when `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` is\n  unset; the per-user home for defaults and profiles;\n- **project-local file** — `iiac-perf.toml` in the current\n  directory (no upward walk); overrides the XDG file field by\n  field, profiles merging by key;\n- **CLI flags** — always win.\n\nThe startup banner's `config` line names the files that were\nloaded (or `none (built-in defaults)`). A present-but-malformed\nfile is a hard error rather than a silent fallback, so a typo\nsurfaces. Every key is optional:\n\n```toml\nduration     = 10.0     # default -d seconds\nband_labels  = \"zpn\"    # zpn | frac | both\ndecimals     = 2        # 0-3\n\n[profiles]              # named --pin core specs\nsmt = \"0,12\"           # SMT siblings of one physical core (contention)\nccx = \"0,1\"            # independent cores, same CCX (best channel latency)\nccd = \"0,6\"            # cross-CCD\n```\n\nEach bench prints a band-based histogram in nanoseconds. Each row\nis one band, labeled by its **upper boundary**, the lower boundary"
}

229	## feat: report options + ps recording
230	
231	Commits: [[14]],[[15]],[[16]],[[17]]
232	
233	Band-label style, decimal display, and picosecond recording —
234	evaluated as CLI options ahead of the config file (options
235	first so we could see how they look; persistence afterwards).
236	Decimals needed ps recording to be uniform: mean/adjusted were
237	f64 already, but first/last/range were integer-ns recorded
238	values until the recording unit became ps.
239	
240	- `--band-labels zpn|frac|both` (default both): labels and
241	  boundary fractions generated in the new `src/bands.rs` from
242	  one structural description (Z_DEPTH/N_DEPTH + deciles), so
243	  the styles can't drift; README's ladder table is pinned by
244	  the module's test. Header records `labels=<style>`.
245	- Picosecond recording: dividing a sample by `inner` in ps
246	  keeps true sub-ns per-call precision — zcr-spin-1t's body
247	  resolved into 4.8/5.2/5.5/5.9 ns rows that were two integer
248	  bins, and its trimmed adjusted mean dropped 5 → 4.6 ns
249	  (ns-rounding inflation removed).
250	- `--decimals N` (0–3, default 1) controls time-column digits;
251	  0 restores integers, 3 is the recording floor.
252	
253	### As-built ladder
254	
255	- 0.14.0-0 `chore: open report options cycle`
256	- 0.14.0-1 `feat: report option --band-labels`
257	- 0.14.0-2 `feat: report picosecond recording`
258	- 0.14.0-3 `feat: report option --decimals`
259	- 0.14.0 `feat: report options + ps recording` — close-out
260	
261	The -2/-3 order was swapped from the original plan (decimals
262	were to precede ps) so the display default could be designed
263	against the real post-ps precision; the 1-digit default then
264	landed in -2 itself to make the ps gain visible immediately.
265	
266	# References
267	
268	[1]: https://github.com/winksaville/iiac-perf/commit/8aaccf8518c4 "8aaccf8518c4cb46bcc2fbf96a317d5d4c962f68"

> AGENT

The `p10` first row isn't fast samples going missing — it's a consequence of how bands are populated combined with how lumpy this workload is.

## The mechanism (`harness.rs:384-398`)

The report doesn't slice by rank. It walks each **recorded histogram bucket** (a distinct value) and drops the *whole* bucket into one band, chosen by the bucket's **midpoint rank**:

```
mid_rank = (cumulative + count/2) / sample_count
idx      = first boundary whose pct > mid_rank
```

A band row prints only if it caught at least one bucket (`band_count[i] == 0` → skipped, line 413). A bucket is never split across bands.

## Applied to your run

`zcr-spin-1t` resolves to a handful of discrete values. The smallest is **5.2 ns**, and it alone holds **98,954** of the 4,832,761 samples. Its midpoint rank:

```
(0 + 98,954/2) / 4,832,761  =  49,477 / 4,832,761  ≈  0.01024
```

Now walk the sub-p10 boundaries — `z4`=0.0001, `z3`=0.001, `z2`=0.01, `p10`=0.10:

- 0.01024 is **not** < 0.0001, 0.001, or 0.01 …
- it **is** < 0.10 → the 5.2 ns bucket lands in the **p10** band.

So `z4`/`z3`/`z2` catch no bucket and are suppressed; `p10` is the first non-empty band. And indeed the `p10` row shows `first == last == 5.2 ns`, `range 0.0` — a single bucket.

## Why it "just missed"

The fast tail (`z4`/`z3`/`z2`, the sub-1% region) can only populate if some bucket's *midpoint rank* falls below 0.01 — i.e. the fastest discrete value must account for less than ~1% of samples. Here the fastest value is ~2% of the run, and its midpoint sits at 1.024% — a hair past the `z2` = 1% line. Had 5.2 ns held fewer than ~94,000 samples (mid-rank < 0.01), it would have landed in `z2` and you'd have seen a `z2` row instead.

This is the flip side of what the README notes for the slow tail ("a band only prints when it has samples"): a distribution this concentrated at its floor can't subdivide its *fast* tail, because one indivisible bucket already overshoots the z-boundaries. The `-d 1` short run doesn't change this — even at `-d 30` the floor bucket stays ~2%, so `p10` remains the first row for this bench.

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/iiac-perf.toml.example",
  "content": "# iiac-perf configuration — sample showing every key at its\n# built-in default.\n#\n# Copy to one of (nearer file wins, field by field):\n#   $XDG_CONFIG_HOME/iiac-perf/config.toml   per-user; ~/.config/\n#                                            iiac-perf/config.toml if\n#                                            XDG_CONFIG_HOME is unset\n#   ./iiac-perf.toml                         project-local, current\n#                                            directory only (no\n#                                            upward walk)\n#\n# Precedence, lowest to highest:\n#   built-in defaults < XDG file < project-local file < CLI flags\n#\n# Every key is optional; an omitted key keeps the built-in default\n# shown here. A present-but-malformed file is a hard error (a typo\n# surfaces rather than silently reverting to defaults). The startup\n# banner's `config` line names the files that were loaded.\n\n# Target wall-clock seconds per bench — the `-d` / `--duration`\n# default. Any positive number. `-d` on the CLI overrides it; `-D`\n# (total budget split per bench) also overrides it.\nduration = 5.0\n\n# Histogram band-label style — the `--band-labels` default. One of:\n#   \"zpn\"  nines/zeros + decile names        (z3, p50, n4)\n#   \"frac\" literal boundary fractions        (0.001, 0.50, 0.999_9)\n#   \"both\" zpn and fraction side by side     (teaches the vocabulary)\nband_labels = \"both\"\n\n# Decimal digits on the report's time columns — the `--decimals`\n# default. Integer 0 to 3:\n#   0  integer nanoseconds\n#   1  sub-ns precision that picosecond recording captures\n#   3  the recording floor (more digits would be artifacts)\ndecimals = 1\n\n# Named pin profiles: each maps a name to a `--pin` core spec, so\n# `--pin <name>` expands to that spec. A `--pin` value that is not a\n# profile name is parsed directly as a raw core spec, so raw specs\n# keep working. Core specs are comma-separated lists with optional\n# ranges: \"0,1\", \"0-5\", \"0,3-5,7\".\n#\n# No profiles are defined by default. The examples below are for an\n# AMD Zen 2 Ryzen 9 3900X, where logical CPUs N and N+12 are SMT\n# siblings of one physical core — adjust to your own topology\n# (`lscpu -e`, or\n# /sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu0/topology/thread_siblings_list).\n#\n# [profiles]\n# smt = \"0,12\"   # SMT siblings of one physical core (max contention)\n# ccx = \"0,1\"    # independent cores, same CCX (best channel latency)\n# ccd = \"0,6\"    # cross-CCD\n"
}

> AGENT

Done. Added `iiac-perf.toml.example` — a ready-to-copy sample with every key at its built-in default, each documented with meaning and possible values:

- **`duration = 5.0`** — `-d` default; any positive number.
- **`band_labels = "both"`** — `zpn` / `frac` / `both`.
- **`decimals = 1`** — integer 0–3 (0 = integer ns, 3 = recording floor).
- **`[profiles]`** — commented examples (`smt`/`ccx`/`ccd`), since there are no default profiles; explains raw-spec fallback and the 3900X topology caveat.

The `.example` suffix keeps it from being auto-loaded when you run from the repo root. Verified it loads clean (banner shows `config iiac-perf.toml`, `labels=both`). Also linked it from the README Config file section and noted it in the chores narrative.

The feature's still uncommitted and ready for your review whenever — the sample file just joined the same change.

1	//! Two-threaded zc-ring-x1 round-trip bench, raw API tier,
2	//! spin waits.
3	
4	use std::hint::black_box;
5	use std::thread;
6	
7	use zc_ring_x1::{Consumer, Empty, Full, Producer};
8	
9	use crate::benches::zcr_common::{Msg, STOP, leak_ring};
10	use crate::harness::{self, Bench, RunCfg};
11	use crate::pin;
12	
13	/// Registry name used on the CLI.
14	pub const NAME: &str = "zcr-raw-2t";
15	
16	/// Main → worker → main round-trip over two zc-ring-x1 rings
17	/// (req + resp), both ends using the raw `reserve_slot` tier
18	/// with hand-written spin loops on `Full`/`Empty`.
19	///
20	/// - Same wait policy as `mpsc-2t-spin` / `ice-*-2t`
21	///   (non-blocking attempt + `spin_loop`), so zcr-raw-2t vs
22	///   those is a pure transport comparison, while zcr-raw-2t vs
23	///   `zcr-with-2t` / `zcr-spin-2t` isolates API-tier cost under
24	///   real cross-core traffic.
25	/// - Shutdown: `Drop` sends the [`STOP`] sentinel; the worker
26	///   exits on receipt without replying.
27	pub struct ZcrRaw2Thread {
28	    req_tx: Producer<'static>,
29	    resp_rx: Consumer<'static>,
30	    worker: Option<thread::JoinHandle<()>>,
31	    counter: u64,
32	}
33	
34	impl ZcrRaw2Thread {
35	    /// Spawn the spinning echo worker over two fresh leaked
36	    /// rings, optionally pinning it to `worker_cpu`.
37	    pub fn new(worker_cpu: Option<usize>) -> Self {
38	        let (req_tx, mut req_rx) = leak_ring();
39	        let (mut resp_tx, resp_rx) = leak_ring();
40	        let worker = thread::spawn(move || {
41	            pin::pin_current(worker_cpu);
42	            loop {
43	                let v = loop {
44	                    match req_rx.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {
45	                        Ok(slot) => {
46	                            let v = *slot;
47	                            slot.release();
48	                            break v;
49	                        }
50	                        Err(Empty) => core::hint::spin_loop(),
51	                    }
52	                };
53	                if v == STOP {
54	                    break;
55	                }
56	                let mut slot = loop {
57	                    match resp_tx.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {
58	                        Ok(slot) => break slot,
59	                        Err(Full) => core::hint::spin_loop(),
60	                    }
61	                };
62	                *slot = v;
63	                slot.commit();
64	            }
65	        });
66	        Self {
67	            req_tx,
68	            resp_rx,
69	            worker: Some(worker),
70	            counter: 0,
71	        }
72	    }
73	}
74	
75	impl Bench for ZcrRaw2Thread {
76	    fn name(&self) -> &str {
77	        "zcr-raw-2t: zc-ring-x1 raw round-trip (2 threads, spin)"
78	    }
79	
80	    fn step(&mut self) -> u64 {
81	        self.counter = self.counter.wrapping_add(1);
82	        if self.counter == STOP {
83	            self.counter = 1;
84	        }
85	        let mut slot = self
86	            .req_tx
87	            .reserve_slot::<Msg>()
88	            .expect("never full: one message in flight");
89	        *slot = self.counter;
90	        slot.commit();
91	        loop {
92	            match self.resp_rx.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {
93	                Ok(slot) => {
94	                    let v = *slot;
95	                    slot.release();
96	                    return black_box(v);
97	                }
98	                Err(Empty) => core::hint::spin_loop(),
99	            }
100	        }
101	    }
102	}
103	
104	impl Drop for ZcrRaw2Thread {
105	    /// Send [`STOP`] and join the worker.
106	    fn drop(&mut self) {
107	        let mut slot = self
108	            .req_tx
109	            .reserve_slot::<Msg>()
110	            .expect("never full: nothing in flight at drop");
111	        *slot = STOP;
112	        slot.commit();
113	        if let Some(worker) = self.worker.take() {
114	            let _ = worker.join();
115	        }
116	    }
117	}
118	
119	/// Registry entry point.
120	pub fn run(cfg: &RunCfg) {
121	    let mut bench = ZcrRaw2Thread::new(cfg.core_for(1));
122	    let (hist, outer, inner, duration_s, suspended_s) = harness::run_adaptive(&mut bench, cfg);
123	    harness::print_report(
124	        bench.name(),
125	        outer,
126	        inner,
127	        duration_s,
128	        &hist,
129	        cfg,
130	        suspended_s,
131	    );
132	}
133	
```

1	//! Two-threaded zc-ring-x1 round-trip bench, closure
2	//! (`reserve_slot_with`) API tier, spin waits.
3	
4	use std::hint::black_box;
5	use std::thread;
6	
7	use zc_ring_x1::{Consumer, Producer};
8	
9	use crate::benches::zcr_common::{Msg, STOP, leak_ring};
10	use crate::harness::{self, Bench, RunCfg};
11	use crate::pin;
12	
13	/// Registry name used on the CLI.
14	pub const NAME: &str = "zcr-with-2t";
15	
16	/// Main → worker → main round-trip over two zc-ring-x1 rings as
17	/// `zcr-raw-2t`, but both ends wait inside `reserve_slot_with`
18	/// with an app-supplied spin closure instead of a hand-written
19	/// retry loop around `reserve_slot`.
20	///
21	/// - Same wait policy as `zcr-raw-2t` (spin_loop hint per failed
22	///   attempt), so any delta between the two is the cost of the
23	///   `_with` wrapper under real cross-core traffic.
24	/// - Shutdown: `Drop` sends the [`STOP`] sentinel; the worker
25	///   exits on receipt without replying.
26	pub struct ZcrWith2Thread {
27	    req_tx: Producer<'static>,
28	    resp_rx: Consumer<'static>,
29	    worker: Option<thread::JoinHandle<()>>,
30	    counter: u64,
31	}
32	
33	impl ZcrWith2Thread {
34	    /// Spawn the spinning echo worker over two fresh leaked
35	    /// rings, optionally pinning it to `worker_cpu`.
36	    pub fn new(worker_cpu: Option<usize>) -> Self {
37	        let (req_tx, mut req_rx) = leak_ring();
38	        let (mut resp_tx, resp_rx) = leak_ring();
39	        let worker = thread::spawn(move || {
40	            pin::pin_current(worker_cpu);
41	            loop {
42	                let v = {
43	                    let slot = req_rx
44	                        .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {
45	                            core::hint::spin_loop();
46	                            true
47	                        })
48	                        .expect("spin closure never gives up");
49	                    let v = *slot;
50	                    slot.release();
51	                    v
52	                };
53	                if v == STOP {
54	                    break;
55	                }
56	                let mut slot = resp_tx
57	                    .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {
58	                        core::hint::spin_loop();
59	                        true
60	                    })
61	                    .expect("spin closure never gives up");
62	                *slot = v;
63	                slot.commit();
64	            }
65	        });
66	        Self {
67	            req_tx,
68	            resp_rx,
69	            worker: Some(worker),
70	            counter: 0,
71	        }
72	    }
73	}
74	
75	impl Bench for ZcrWith2Thread {
76	    fn name(&self) -> &str {
77	        "zcr-with-2t: zc-ring-x1 reserve_slot_with round-trip (2 threads, spin)"
78	    }
79	
80	    fn step(&mut self) -> u64 {
81	        self.counter = self.counter.wrapping_add(1);
82	        if self.counter == STOP {
83	            self.counter = 1;
84	        }
85	        let mut slot = self
86	            .req_tx
87	            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {
88	                core::hint::spin_loop();
89	                true
90	            })
91	            .expect("spin closure never gives up");
92	        *slot = self.counter;
93	        slot.commit();
94	        let slot = self
95	            .resp_rx
96	            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {
97	                core::hint::spin_loop();
98	                true
99	            })
100	            .expect("spin closure never gives up");
101	        let v = *slot;
102	        slot.release();
103	        black_box(v)
104	    }
105	}
106	
107	impl Drop for ZcrWith2Thread {
108	    /// Send [`STOP`] and join the worker.
109	    fn drop(&mut self) {
110	        let mut slot = self
111	            .req_tx
112	            .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {
113	                core::hint::spin_loop();
114	                true
115	            })
116	            .expect("spin closure never gives up");
117	        *slot = STOP;
118	        slot.commit();
119	        if let Some(worker) = self.worker.take() {
120	            let _ = worker.join();
121	        }
122	    }
123	}
124	
125	/// Registry entry point.
126	pub fn run(cfg: &RunCfg) {
127	    let mut bench = ZcrWith2Thread::new(cfg.core_for(1));
128	    let (hist, outer, inner, duration_s, suspended_s) = harness::run_adaptive(&mut bench, cfg);
129	    harness::print_report(
130	        bench.name(),
131	        outer,
132	        inner,
133	        duration_s,
134	        &hist,
135	        cfg,
136	        suspended_s,
137	    );
138	}
139
