```
60	    ///   holds the `&mut Consumer` borrow, so a second
61	    ///   `reserve_slot` before the guard is dropped or released
62	    ///   does not compile.
63	    /// - Dropping the guard without [`ReadSlot::release`]
64	    ///   leaves the slot unread — the next `reserve_slot`
65	    ///   returns it again.
66	    pub fn reserve_slot<T>(&mut self) -> Result<ReadSlot<'_, T>, Empty>
67	    where
68	        T: FromBytes + KnownLayout + Immutable,
69	    {
70	        check_type::<T>(self.slot_size);
71	        let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
72	        let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);
73	        if p == c {
74	            return Err(Empty);
75	        }
76	        // Raw pointer, not `&T`, for the same reason as in the
77	        // producer's reserve_slot: release's store frees the
78	        // slot for producer writes while a reference field
79	        // would still be argument-protected.
80	        let msg = slot_ptr(self.slots, c, self.mask, self.slot_size) as *const T;
81	        Ok(ReadSlot {
82	            header: self.header,
83	            msg,
84	            next_idx: c.wrapping_add(1),
85	            _slot: PhantomData,
86	        })
87	    }
88	
89	    /// [`reserve_slot`](Consumer::reserve_slot) with an
90	    /// injected wait policy: retry until a message arrives or
91	    /// the policy gives up.
92	    ///
93	    /// - `on_empty` is called after each failed attempt with
94	    ///   the attempt count (0-based, saturating); returning
95	    ///   `false` gives up → `Err(Empty)`.
96	    /// - The wait loop is the reservation itself (the guard
97	    ///   borrows the endpoint, so retrying a `reserve_slot`
98	    ///   call could not return it): `consumer_idx` is ours
99	    ///   and loaded once; only `producer_idx` is re-read per
100	    ///   attempt, so the no-wait fast path does exactly the
101	    ///   loads `reserve_slot` does.
102	    /// - See [`policy`](crate::policy) for shipped policies
103	    ///   and the composition model.
104	    pub fn reserve_slot_with<T>(
105	        &mut self,
106	        mut on_empty: impl FnMut(u32) -> bool,
107	    ) -> Result<ReadSlot<'_, T>, Empty>
108	    where
109	        T: FromBytes + KnownLayout + Immutable,
110	    {
111	        check_type::<T>(self.slot_size);
112	        // Ours alone: no peer moves it, load once.
113	        let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
114	        let mut attempt = 0u32;
115	        loop {
116	            let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);
117	            if p != c {
118	                break;
119	            }
120	            if !on_empty(attempt) {
121	                return Err(Empty);
122	            }
123	            attempt = attempt.saturating_add(1);
124	        }
125	        // Raw pointer, not `&T` — see reserve_slot.
126	        let msg = slot_ptr(self.slots, c, self.mask, self.slot_size) as *const T;
127	        Ok(ReadSlot {
128	            header: self.header,
129	            msg,
130	            next_idx: c.wrapping_add(1),
131	            _slot: PhantomData,
132	        })
133	    }
134	
135	    /// [`reserve_slot`](Consumer::reserve_slot), spinning
136	    /// until a message arrives —
137	    /// [`reserve_slot_with`](Consumer::reserve_slot_with)
138	    /// under the never-quitting
139	    /// [`policy::spin`](crate::policy::spin), so no `Result`.
140	    pub fn reserve_slot_spin<T>(&mut self) -> ReadSlot<'_, T>
141	    where
142	        T: FromBytes + KnownLayout + Immutable,
143	    {
144	        match self.reserve_slot_with(crate::policy::spin) {
145	            Ok(slot) => slot,
146	            Err(Empty) => unreachable!("policy::spin never gives up"),
147	        }
148	    }
149	}
150	
151	/// A reserved read slot: `Deref` to read the message, then
152	/// [`release`](ReadSlot::release).
153	pub struct ReadSlot<'c, T> {
154	    /// The ring's control block (for the release store).
155	    header: &'c Header,
156	    /// The slot, viewed as the message type. Raw on purpose —
157	    /// see the comment in [`Consumer::reserve_slot`].
158	    msg: *const T,
159	    /// Value `consumer_idx` takes on release.
160	    next_idx: u32,
161	    /// Owns the `&'c` borrow of the consumer.
162	    _slot: PhantomData<&'c T>,
163	}
164	
165	impl<T> Deref for ReadSlot<'_, T> {
166	    type Target = T;
167	    /// Read access to the in-slot message.
168	    fn deref(&self) -> &T {
169	        // SAFETY: msg is in-bounds and aligned (check_type +
170	        // cache-line slot base), any byte pattern is a valid T
171	        // (FromBytes bound at reserve_slot), and the index
172	        // protocol gives this guard read access until release.
173	        unsafe { &*self.msg }
174	    }
175	}
176	
177	impl<T> ReadSlot<'_, T> {
178	    /// Free the slot for reuse (`consumer_idx + 1`, `Release`).
179	    pub fn release(self) {
180	        self.header
181	            .consumer_idx
182	            .store(self.next_idx, Ordering::Release);
183	    }
184	}
185	
```

> AGENT

Not a bug, and not DCE eating your workload — this is the tiers behaving exactly as designed, and your run surfaced it cleanly. Here's what's going on.

## The one real difference between the tiers

All three do the identical thing on the *send* side and on the *fast* (message-already-present) path. They differ only in the **wait loop on the receive side**:

- **raw** (`zcr_raw_2t.rs:91-100`) is the naive "call the fallible API in a loop" pattern:
  ```rust
  loop { match self.resp_rx.reserve_slot::<Msg>() { Ok(..)=>.., Err(Empty)=>spin_loop() } }
  ```
  Every iteration re-runs `reserve_slot` (`consumer.rs:71-72`), which loads **both** indices: `consumer_idx` (Relaxed) *and* `producer_idx` (Acquire).

- **with** / **spin** call `reserve_slot_with` (`consumer.rs:104-133`), whose whole point is to hoist the caller-owned index out of the retry loop. Its own doc says it:

  > `consumer_idx` is ours and loaded once; only `producer_idx` is re-read per attempt.

So on the waiting side, **raw redundantly re-loads its own `consumer_idx` on every spin; with/spin load it once and spin only on the peer's `producer_idx`.** That's the entire code delta.

And `reserve_slot_spin` *is* `reserve_slot_with(policy::spin)` (`consumer.rs:144`) — which is why **with and spin are statistically identical** (mean 114.8 vs 115.0, trimmed stdev 7.46 vs 7.19). They're the same code path with a different-shaped closure.

## Why the redundant load costs you ~30 ns and widens the body

Two facts rule out the usual suspects:
- `producer_idx` and `consumer_idx` are each `CacheAligned` on **separate cache lines** (`lib.rs:78-79`, "Header spanning four cache lines"), so this is **not** false sharing between the two indices.
- The extra load is Relaxed and on main's *own* line — cheap in isolation.

**We think** the mechanism is: a relaxed **atomic** load can't be hoisted or coalesced out of a loop by LLVM (atomics are treated conservatively), so raw genuinely issues that second load every spin. That makes each spin iteration longer and, more importantly, changes *when* main samples the peer's `producer_idx` relative to the worker's `commit` store landing. The wait is a tight race against a cross-core store becoming visible; a looser, longer spin body samples that store later and less regularly — so you get both a higher mean and a *multi-modal* body (raw's 120/129/130/140/150 in ~10 ns steps ≈ one Zen2 cross-core coherence hop each, vs with/spin collapsing to 100/109/110). The tails (`n3`+) are dominated by scheduler/OS effects and look ~the same across all three, as expected.

That is precisely the cost this bench family was built to expose — your doc comment on `zcr_raw_2t.rs:22-24` frames it as "isolates API-tier cost under real cross-core traffic." raw = the hand-rolled retry that re-reads the owned index; `_with`/`_spin` = the same retry with that read hoisted.

## To confirm it's the extra load and not something else

A couple of cheap checks if you want certainty:
- **Disassembly**: `objdump`/`cargo asm` the two `step()` inner loops — raw's should show two `mov`s from the header (both indices), with/spin one. That nails the "non-hoistable extra load" claim directly.
- **perf**: `perf stat -e mem_inst_retired.all_loads,cache-references iiac-perf -d 10 zcr-raw-2t` vs `zcr-with-2t` — raw should retire measurably more loads per call.
- **Pin them**: `--pin 0,1` (same-CCX) vs `--pin 0,12` (SMT siblings). If the gap scales with cross-core hop cost, it shrinks on SMT siblings and grows across CCDs — consistent with the coherence-timing story; if it stayed constant it'd argue for pure per-spin instruction cost instead.

Want me to pull the two inner-loop disassemblies to confirm the load count? That's the decisive one.

So Consumer::reserve_slot<T> should call `self.reserve_slot_with<T>(|_| { return false })`

> AGENT

Correct — functionally `reserve_slot` is exactly `reserve_slot_with(|_| false)`:

```rust
pub fn reserve_slot<T>(&mut self) -> Result<ReadSlot<'_, T>, Empty>
where T: FromBytes + KnownLayout + Immutable {
    self.reserve_slot_with(|_| false)   // give up after the first failed attempt
}
```

Walking `reserve_slot_with` with `on_empty = |_| false`: load `consumer_idx` once, load `producer_idx` once, `p == c` → `on_empty(0)` returns `false` → `Err(Empty)`; `p != c` → build the slot → `Ok`. Same two loads, same orderings (Relaxed then Acquire), same result. It's a clean dedup, and the `_with` doc already asserts the fast path "does exactly the loads `reserve_slot` does," so this just makes that literal. The `|_| false` closure trivially constant-folds, so standalone callers should get byte-identical codegen (worth a quick disasm check to be sure).

Two things to keep clear, though:

**1. This does *not* change the benchmark finding.** raw's extra per-spin `consumer_idx` load doesn't come from *inside* `reserve_slot` — it comes from the **bench looping over it**:

```rust
loop { match self.resp_rx.reserve_slot::<Msg>() { Ok(..)=>.., Err(Empty)=>spin_loop() } }
```

Each call reloads `consumer_idx` no matter how `reserve_slot` is implemented. The only way to hoist that load out of the retry is to move the loop *into* the reservation — which is precisely `reserve_slot_with`/`reserve_slot_spin`. So after this dedup, `zcr-raw-2t` would still measure the same ~130 ns body. The tier difference is inherent to "retry a single-shot call" vs "retry inside the call," and keeping raw as the hand-rolled loop is the whole point of the comparison.

**2. This edit lives in `../zc-ring-x1`, a separate repo.** It's out of scope for the config-file change currently sitting uncommitted here. It's a good library simplification (removes ~15 duplicated lines each in `consumer.rs` and `producer.rs`, single source of truth for the reservation protocol) — but it belongs in that crate's own cycle.

So it seems we should remove Consumer::reserve_slot?

> AGENT

I'd push back on removing it — keep the method, just reimplement it via `reserve_slot_with`. "Redundant as an implementation" isn't the same as "redundant as API," and here the public method earns its keep for three reasons:

**1. It's the ergonomic non-blocking primitive.** `Result<Slot, Empty>` try-once is the idiomatic building block for composing a ring into *someone else's* wait strategy — an epoll/select loop, a custom backoff, a "drain what's ready then go do other work" scheduler. Those callers want:
```rust
if let Ok(slot) = c.reserve_slot::<Msg>() { ... }   // try once, move on
```
Forcing every such site to spell it `reserve_slot_with(|_| false)` is pure noise — a closure whose only job is to say "don't loop," on a method whose defining feature is that it doesn't loop. The named method *is* the readable form of that intent.

**2. Removing it is a breaking API change that kills the raw tier.** `zcr-raw-2t/1t` call `reserve_slot` directly precisely to measure the naive "retry a single-shot call" pattern — the pattern that produced the finding you just found interesting. Delete the method and that whole tier of the comparison goes with it; you'd be removing the subject of the experiment.

**3. The three-tier API is a deliberate ladder**, and `reserve_slot` is its base rung:
- `reserve_slot` — try once, you handle the miss (compose into your own loop).
- `reserve_slot_with` — retry under an injected policy.
- `reserve_slot_spin` — retry forever (`_with(policy::spin)`).

That's a coherent progression from "most control" to "most convenience." Each rung is a real use case, not an accident.

So the right move is the dedup we discussed — `reserve_slot { self.reserve_slot_with(|_| false) }` (and the `Producer` mirror) — which removes the *duplicated protocol code* while keeping the *named entry point*. You lose the copy-paste, not the API.

The one case where I'd actually remove it: if `reserve_slot` had **no callers and no external users** and you'd decided a non-blocking try wasn't a use case you want to support. But it has callers (the raw benches), and "try once" is a normal thing to want from a ring. So: reimplement, don't remove.

You've put your finger on the real flaw in my argument: the raw bench isn't a *usage* model, it's an *anti-pattern* model. Looping on a single-shot `reserve_slot` is exactly the mistake — it's `reserve_slot_spin` written by hand, badly (no hoisting). So "the raw bench needs it" is a bad reason to keep the method. Point taken.

That reframes the question but doesn't quite settle it. The honest fork is:

**Is "try once, non-blocking" a use case you want to bless in the public API?** — because that's the *only* thing `reserve_slot` offers that `_with`/`_spin` don't. Not "retry cheaply" (that's `_with`), not "spin" (`_spin`) — just "probe once, hand me `Err(Empty)`, I'll go do something else." That's a real pattern (drive the ring from an external epoll/select loop, drain-what's-ready-then-yield), but it's a *different* shape from this bench family, which is all ping-pong retry.

So:

- **If you don't have that external-event-loop use case in mind** → remove `reserve_slot`. `reserve_slot_with(|_| false)` covers the rare non-blocking probe, and the two-rung API (`_with` primitive + `_spin` convenience) is tighter and more honest. This is the minimalist-WIP-perf answer, and I think it's defensible.

- **If you do want to bless non-blocking-try** → keep a method, but rename it `try_reserve_slot` (mirrors `Mutex::try_lock`, `Sender::try_send`) so the name states the semantics, and implement it as `reserve_slot_with(|_| false)`. `reserve_slot` is a poor name anyway — it doesn't say "fails immediately."

Either way, **the raw bench should stop pretending to be usage.** Two clean options for it:
- **Drop the raw tier** — keep `with`/`spin` as the sanctioned tiers. Simplest.
- **Keep it, relabeled** as "cost of the hand-rolled retry anti-pattern" (loop on `_with(|_| false)`), as a cautionary data point. It *is* a striking result — +30 ns and multimodal from one non-hoisted load — and there's teaching value in that.

My lean: **remove `reserve_slot`** (you don't seem to have the external-event-loop consumer, and `_with(|_| false)` is the escape hatch), and **drop the raw benches** rather than keep an anti-pattern around — unless you find the "naive retry costs you 30 ns" data compelling enough to keep as a labeled cautionary bench.

Which way do you want to go on the API — remove outright, or rename to `try_reserve_slot`? And do you want the raw benches dropped or relabeled? That decides the edits, and note it all lands in `../zc-ring-x1` (+ these benches here), separate from the config work still uncommitted in this repo.

> AGENT

Agreed, and your "never worse, sometimes better" is exactly right — `reserve_slot_with` *dominates* `reserve_slot`:

- **Single probe** (`|_| false`): identical loads — c once, p once, compare, return. Equal. And the `|_| false` loop folds away, so codegen should be byte-identical (worth a disasm confirm).
- **Anything beyond one probe**: `_with` hoists the caller-owned index out of the retry, which `reserve_slot`-in-a-loop can't. So it's strictly better the moment you retry even twice.

And crucially, for the actual Tokio case you're imagining, the *ideal* policy usually isn't pure single-shot either — it's "spin a bounded N times, then return pending and let the runtime park you" (a cheap hybrid that avoids a full park on the common near-ready case). That's `reserve_slot_with(|attempt| attempt < N)` — which again hoists the owned load across those N spins. So `_with` isn't just the non-blocking escape hatch, it's the *better* building block for the very integration that motivates wanting non-blocking. That reinforces removal rather than undercuts it.

**Producer side: yes, exactly symmetric.** `Producer::reserve_slot` loads `producer_idx` (own) + `consumer_idx` (peer) each call; `reserve_slot_with` loads the owned `producer_idx` once and re-reads only `consumer_idx`. Same domination, same reasoning. Remove both.

So the resulting API is a clean two-rung ladder on each endpoint:
- `reserve_slot_with(policy)` — the fallible primitive (non-blocking = `|_| false`, hybrid = bounded policy, …).
- `reserve_slot_spin()` — the ergonomic forever-spin convenience (`_with(policy::spin)`).

No first-class non-blocking method for now (YAGNI — you have no consumer). If a real Tokio integration later makes `reserve_slot_with(|_| false)` show up all over, a two-line `try_reserve_slot` alias (mirroring `Sender::try_send`) is trivial to add then, named to actually say "fails immediately." Don't pre-build it.

**Consequence for the benches** — they call `reserve_slot` directly, so removal forces the question you already answered: the raw tier is an anti-pattern, not usage. I'd **drop `zcr-raw-1t/2t`** entirely rather than rewrite them to loop on `_with(|_| false)` (which would just be the same anti-pattern in disguise). Keep `with`/`spin` as the sanctioned tiers. If the "+30 ns from a non-hoisted load" result feels worth preserving, capture it as a one-paragraph note in the chores/README rather than a live bench.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01BV7mpRUXik85BkjTjkjVCU
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/zc-ring-x1/notes/bugs.md",
  "old_string": "## Bugs\n\n\n# References",
  "new_string": "## Bugs\n\n1. `reserve_slot` (both `Consumer` and `Producer`) is a strictly\n   dominated API rung — `reserve_slot_with` does everything it does,\n   never worse and sometimes better — and its naive \"loop on the\n   fallible call\" usage carries a measurable latency + jitter cost.\n   Consider removing both.\n   - **What we measured.** iiac-perf's zcr round-trip benches\n     (`-d 60`, unpinned 3900X, one message in flight) compare the\n     tiers under real cross-core traffic. `zcr-raw-2t` (main loops on\n     `reserve_slot` while waiting for the reply) lands a body mean\n     ~130 ns, multimodal across ~120–150 ns, trimmed `min..n2` stdev\n     ~15 ns. `zcr-with-2t` / `zcr-spin-2t` (same wait via\n     `reserve_slot_with` / `reserve_slot_spin`) land ~110 ns, body\n     clustered at 100/109/110 ns, trimmed stdev ~7 ns. So the raw\n     retry pattern costs ~+30 ns and ~2× the body jitter. `with` and\n     `spin` are statistically identical, as expected —\n     `reserve_slot_spin` *is* `reserve_slot_with(policy::spin)`.\n   - **Why (we think).** The only code difference on the wait path is\n     that `reserve_slot`-in-a-loop re-reads the caller-*owned* index\n     (`consumer_idx` on the read side, `producer_idx` on the write\n     side) on every iteration, because each call reloads it;\n     `reserve_slot_with` loads the owned index once and re-reads only\n     the peer's index per attempt (its doc already states this).\n     A relaxed atomic load can't be hoisted/coalesced out of a loop\n     by LLVM, so the naive retry genuinely issues the extra load each\n     spin. We think that lengthens and de-tightens the spin, so it\n     samples the peer's store later and less regularly — a higher\n     mean and a multimodal body in ~10 ns steps (≈ one Zen 2\n     cross-core coherence hop). The two indices are each\n     `CacheAligned` on separate lines, so this is **not** false\n     sharing between them.\n   - **Current thinking.** `reserve_slot` ≡\n     `reserve_slot_with(|_| false)` (give up after the first failed\n     attempt) — identical loads, identical result, and the `|_| false`\n     loop folds away (worth a disasm confirm). For any retry beyond\n     one probe, `_with` hoists the owned load and wins. The desirable\n     always-non-blocking mode (e.g. driving the ring from a Tokio\n     task) is `reserve_slot_with(|_| false)`; the better hybrid\n     (\"spin a bounded N, then return pending and let the runtime\n     park\") is `reserve_slot_with(|attempt| attempt < N)` — both\n     things `reserve_slot` can't express. So the proposed shape is a\n     two-rung ladder per endpoint: `reserve_slot_with` (fallible\n     primitive) + `reserve_slot_spin` (forever-spin convenience),\n     dropping `reserve_slot`. If a real consumer later makes\n     `_with(|_| false)` common, add a thin `try_reserve_slot` alias\n     (named like `Sender::try_send`) then — not before (no consumers\n     exist yet).\n   - **Downstream.** iiac-perf's `zcr-raw-1t` / `zcr-raw-2t` call\n     `reserve_slot` directly and only exist to measure this naive\n     pattern; removing the method drops (or relabels) those benches.\n     Tracked on the iiac-perf side.\n\n# References"
}
```
